To proceed, we need the interior product of exterior algebra.

Definition 7.1
.

Take two vectors
\alpha\in\bigwedge^{p+1}\mathbb{C}^{n+1}
and
\beta\in\bigwedge^{q+1}(\mathbb{C}^{n+1})^{\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vee$}}
, where
0\leqslant q\leqslant p\leqslant n
.
The interior product
\alpha\,\llcorner\,\beta
is defined to be the unique vector in
\bigwedge^{p-q}\mathbb{C}^{n+1}
satisfying

(7.4)

\gamma(\alpha\,\llcorner\,\beta)=(\beta\wedge\gamma)(\alpha),

for all
\gamma\in\bigwedge^{p-q}(\mathbb{C}^{n+1})^{\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vee$}}
.
In the particular case where
p=q
, one has
\alpha\,\llcorner\,\beta=\beta(\alpha)
.

Observe that each global section
\sigma\in H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{n},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{n}}(1))
can be regarded as a linear functional of
\mathbb{C}^{n+1}
, which amounts to the isomorphism
H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{n},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{n}}(1))\cong(\mathbb{C}^{n+1})^{\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vee$}}
.
More concretely, for a reduced representation
[x_{0}:\dots:x_{n}]
of
x\in\mathbb{P}^{n}
, one can write
\sigma(x)=a_{0}x_{0}+\dots+a_{n}x_{n}
, which implies that
\sigma
can be represented by
\bm{a}=(a_{0},\dots,a_{n})\in(\mathbb{C}^{n+1})^{\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vee$}}
.
For a hyperplane
H=(\sigma)
, one sees that
\bm{a}
is indeed a normal vector of
H
.
Let
H
be the hyperplane with a unit normal vector
\bm{a}
.
Thereupon the
k
-th contact function of
f
for
H
is defined by

\phi_{k}(H)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\frac{|F^{k}\,\llcorner\,\bm{a}|^{2}}{|F^{k}|^{2}},

for each
0\leqslant k\leqslant n
.

Accordingly, the following lemma is known as Ahlfors’ LLD.

Lemma 7.2

(Ahlfors’ LLD)
.

[
Ahl41
]

Let
H
be a hyperplane of
\mathbb{P}^{n}
, and let
f\colon\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{P}^{n}
be a non-constant holomorphic curve such that
f(\mathbb{C})\not\subset H
.
Then for any
0<\varepsilon<1
, one has

(7.5)

\varepsilon\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{\phi_{1}(H)}{\phi_{0}(H)^{1-\varepsilon}}\Omega_{0},r\right)\leqslant(1+\varepsilon)T_{f}(r,\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{n}}(1))+O(1).

As a reminder, a linear subspace of
\mathbb{P}^{n}
is said to be a closed subspace of
\mathbb{P}^{n}
defined by linear homogeneous equations.
For any linear subspace
H^{\ell}
of
\mathbb{P}^{n}
of codimension
\ell+1
, it is easy to find
\ell+1
hyperplanes
H_{0},\dots,H_{\ell}
in
\mathbb{P}^{n}
such that
H^{\ell}=H_{0}\cap\dots\cap H_{\ell}
.
Viewing
H^{\ell}
as a hyperplane in
\textnormal{P}(\bigwedge^{\ell+1}\mathbb{C}^{n+1})
, one sees that
\bm{a}_{0}\wedge\dots\wedge\bm{a}_{\ell}\in\bigwedge^{\ell+1}(\mathbb{C}^{n+1})^{\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vee$}}
is a normal vector of
H^{\ell}
, where
\bm{a}_{i}
is a normal vector of
H_{i}
for
0\leqslant i\leqslant\ell
.
Actually, each linear subspace
H^{\ell}
one-to-one corresponds to a unit normal vector
\alpha\in\bigwedge^{\ell+1}(\mathbb{C}^{n+1})^{\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vee$}}
.
Let
H^{\ell}
be the hyperplane in
\textnormal{P}(\bigwedge^{\ell+1}\mathbb{C}^{n+1})
with a unit normal vector
\alpha\in\bigwedge^{\ell+1}(\mathbb{C}^{n+1})^{\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vee$}}
.
Analogously, the
k
-th contact function of
f
for
H^{\ell}
is defined by

\phi_{k}(H^{\ell})\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\frac{|F^{k}\,\llcorner\,\alpha|^{2}}{|F^{k}|^{2}},

for each
0\leqslant\ell\leqslant k\leqslant n
.

Take a linear subspace
H^{\ell-1}
of
\mathbb{P}^{n}
of codimension
\ell
with a unit normal vector
\beta\in\bigwedge^{\ell}(\mathbb{C}^{n+1})^{\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vee$}}
.
Let
f_{H^{\ell-1}}\colon\mathbb{C}\to\textnormal{P}((H^{\ell-1})^{\perp})\cong\mathbb{P}^{n-\ell}
be the analytic extension of
\pr\circ(F^{\ell}\,\llcorner\,\beta)_{\restriction U}
, where
U=\mathbb{C}\setminus(F^{\ell}\,\llcorner\,\beta)^{-1}(0)
.
Fujimoto showed that
f_{H^{\ell-1}}
is linear non-degenerate if
f
is so (see Proposition 2.6.2 in
[
Fuj93
]
).
And he called it the contracted curve of
f
to the direction
H^{\ell-1}
(see Section 2.6 of
[
Fuj93
]
for more details).

Suppose that
H^{\ell}
is a linear subspace of
\mathbb{P}^{n}
of codimension
\ell+1
such that
H^{\ell}=H^{\ell-1}\cap H_{0}
for some hyperplane
H_{0}
of
\mathbb{P}^{n}
.
Then one can obtains the following formula (see also (2.6.10) in
[
Fuj93
]
):

\phi_{k}(H^{\ell})=\bar{\phi}_{k-\ell}(H_{0})\phi_{k}(H^{\ell-1}),

for each
\ell\leqslant k\leqslant n
, where
\bar{\phi}_{k-\ell}(H_{0})
is the
(k-\ell)
-th contact function of
f_{H^{\ell-1}}
for
H_{0}
.

By induction, one has Ahlfors’ LLD for
(k+1)
-jets intersecting linear subspaces of codimension
\ell+1
.

Lemma 7.3
.

[
Ahl41
]

Let
H^{\ell}
be a complete intersection of
\ell+1
hyperplanes in
\mathbb{P}^{n}
, for
0\leqslant\ell\leqslant n-1
.
Let
f\colon\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{P}^{n}
be a non-constant holomorphic curve such that
f(\mathbb{C})\not\subset H^{\ell}
.
For each
0\leqslant k\leqslant n-1
, suppose that the image
f(\mathbb{C})
does not lie in any linear subspace
\mathbb{P}^{k}\subset\mathbb{P}^{n}
besides.
Then there is a constant
c_{k-\ell}
such that for any
0<\varepsilon<1
, one has

\varepsilon\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{\phi_{k+1}(H^{\ell})}{\phi_{k}(H^{\ell})^{1-\varepsilon}}\Omega_{k},r\right)\leqslant c_{k-\ell}T_{f^{k}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{n_{k}}}(1))+O(1).

7.3.
Maps to the Grassmannian

In the following three sections, we will only consider the case of intersecting divisors for simplicity.

Let
(X,V)
be a directed projective manifold, and choose a linear system
\mathfrak{S}\subset|L|
on
X
.
Suppose that
\mathfrak{S}
is base point free, i.e.,
\Bs(\mathfrak{S})=\varnothing
.
Accordingly,
\mu_{\mathfrak{S}}
defined by (
6.9
) is indeed a holomorphic map as follows.

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathfrak{S}}\colon X

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathfrak{S}^{\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vee$}}\cong\mathbb{P}^{N}

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle\longmapsto[\varsigma_{0}(x):\dots:\varsigma_{N}(x)],

where
N=\dim\mathfrak{S}
,
\{\varsigma_{i}\mid i=0,1,\dots,N\}
is a basis of
\mathbb{S}
, and
\mathbb{S}\subset H^{0}(X,L)
is the linear subspace corresponding to
\mathfrak{S}
.
Obviously, if
L
is very ample and
\mathfrak{S}
is the complete linear system
|L|
, then
\mu_{\mathfrak{S}}
is a closed embedding.

Let
f\colon(\mathbb{C},T_{\mathbb{C}})\to(X,V)
be a non-constant holomorphic curve.
It is evident that
[\varsigma_{0}\circ f:\dots:\varsigma_{N}\circ f]
is a reduced representation of
\tilde{f}
, where
\tilde{f}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\mu_{\mathfrak{S}}\circ f
is a non-constant holomorphic curve in
\mathbb{P}^{N}
.
In the particular case where
(X,V)=(\mathbb{P}^{n},T_{\mathbb{P}^{n}})
and
\mathfrak{S}=|H|
for some hyperplane
H
of
\mathbb{P}^{n}
,
we see that
\mu_{\mathfrak{S}}
is indeed an isomorphism and
[\varsigma_{0}\circ f:\dots:\varsigma_{n}\circ f]
gives a reduced representation of
f
.

Analogous to (
7.1
), we construct a holomorphic curve
\widetilde{F}^{k}
in
\bigwedge^{k+1}\mathbb{C}^{N+1}
associated to
\tilde{f}
as follows.
Take the standard basis
\{\bm{e}^{i}\mid 0\leqslant i\leqslant N\}
of
\mathbb{C}^{N+1}
, namely,
\bm{e}^{i}=(\delta_{1}^{i+1},\dots,\delta_{N+1}^{i+1})\in\mathbb{C}^{N+1}
for each
0\leqslant i\leqslant N
.
Here
\delta_{j}^{i}
is the Kronecker symbol, defined by

\delta_{j}^{i}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\begin{cases}1&i=j,\\
0&i\neq j,\end{cases}

where
i,j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
.
Consequently, we get

\widetilde{F}(z)=\sum_{i=0}^{N}\varsigma_{i}\circ f(z)\bm{e}^{i}.

Moreover,
\{\bm{e}^{i_{0}}\wedge\dots\wedge\bm{e}^{i_{k}}\mid 0\leqslant i_{0}<\dots<i_{k}\leqslant N\}
is also a basis of
\bigwedge^{k+1}\mathbb{C}^{N+1}
.
By computation, we obtain

(7.6)

\widetilde{F}^{k}=\sum_{0\leqslant i_{0}<\dots<i_{k}\leqslant N}\hskip-10.00002ptW(\varsigma_{i_{0}},\dots,\varsigma_{i_{k}})(j_{k}f)\bm{e}^{i_{0}}\wedge\dots\wedge\bm{e}^{i_{k}}.

Inspired by the
k
-th derived curve and (
7.6
), for each
1\leqslant k\leqslant N
, we define a rational map

\displaystyle\varpi_{k,\mathfrak{S}}\colon X_{k}

\displaystyle\dashrightarrow\textnormal{P}(\bigwedge^{k+1}\mathbb{S}^{\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vee$}})\cong\mathbb{P}^{N_{k}}

\displaystyle x_{k}

\displaystyle\longmapsto[\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\varsigma_{i_{0}},\dots,\varsigma_{i_{k}})(x_{k})]_{0\leqslant i_{0}<\dots<i_{k}\leqslant N},

where
N_{k}=\binom{N+1}{k+1}-1
.
Apparently, the indeterminacy locus of
\varpi_{k,\mathfrak{S}}
lies in the base locus
\Bs(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S}))\subset X_{k}
.
Assume that the lifting
f_{[k]}
of
f
to
(X_{k},V_{k})
satisfies that
f_{[k]}(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Bs(\mathfrak{W}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S}))
.
Consider the
k
-th derived curve
\tilde{f}^{k}
of
\tilde{f}
.
It follows from (
7.6
) that

(7.7)

\tilde{f}_{\restriction U}^{k}=\varpi_{k,\mathfrak{S}}\circ f_{[k]\restriction U},

where
U=\mathbb{C}\setminus f_{[k]}^{-1}(\Bs(\mathfrak{W}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})))
.

As mentioned above, the
k
-th derived curve factors through the Plücker embedding into a holomorphic curve in the Grassmannian, which is known as the
k
-th associated curve of
f
.
Likewise, we are going to show that
\varpi_{k,\mathfrak{S}}
factors through the Plücker embedding as well.

Take a fixed trivialization of
L
on an admissible open subset
U\subset X
.
By Corollary
2.3
, there exists an open subset
U_{k}\subset\pi_{0,k}^{-1}(U)
, which allows us to take a germ of curves
f_{x_{k}}
tangent to
V
for every
x_{k}\in U_{k}
, depending holomorphically on
x_{k}
, such that
(f_{x_{k}})_{[k]}(0)=x_{k}
and
(f_{x_{k}})^{\prime}_{[k-1]}(0)\neq 0
.
For any
0\leqslant\ell\leqslant k
, we denote

\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}_{U,\mathfrak{S}}^{[\ell]}(x_{k})\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\left(\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[\ell]}\varsigma_{i,U}(j_{k}f_{x_{k}})\right)_{0\leqslant i\leqslant N}=\left((\varsigma_{i,U}\circ f_{x_{k}})^{(\ell)}(0)\right)_{0\leqslant i\leqslant N}\in\mathbb{S}^{\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vee$}}.

Consider the rational map

\displaystyle\mu_{U_{k},\mathfrak{S}}\colon U_{k}

\displaystyle\dashrightarrow\Gr(k,\mathfrak{S}^{\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vee$}})\cong\Gr(k+1,N+1)

\displaystyle x_{k}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\Span\left(\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}_{U,\mathfrak{S}}^{[0]}(x_{k}),\dots,\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}_{U,\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]}(x_{k})\right).

The dependence on the choice of
f_{x_{k}}
and the trivialization of
L_{\restriction U}
notwithstanding, we have

\varpi_{k,\mathfrak{S}\restriction U_{k}}=\Pluc\circ\mu_{U_{k},\mathfrak{S}},

where
\Pluc\colon\Gr(k+1,N+1)\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{N_{k}}
is, by abuse of notation, the Plücker embedding.
This verifies that
\varpi_{k,\mathfrak{S}}
factors through the Plücker embedding into a rational map

\mu_{k,\mathfrak{S}}\colon X_{k}\dashrightarrow\Gr(k,\mathfrak{S}^{\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vee$}})\cong\Gr(k+1,N+1).

From (
7.2
) and (
7.7
), we immediately get

(7.8)

\tilde{f}_{k\restriction U}=\mu_{k,\mathfrak{S}}\circ f_{[k]\restriction U},

where
\tilde{f}_{k}
is the
k
-th associated curve of
\tilde{f}
, and
U
is defined above.

Furthermore, observe that
\Bs(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S}))\neq\varnothing
holds for each
k\geqslant 2
.
Take the blow-up of
X_{k}
with respect to
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})

\upsilon_{k,\mathfrak{S}}\colon\widehat{X}_{k,\mathfrak{S}}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\Bl_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})}(X_{k})\longrightarrow X_{k}.

Let
E_{k,\mathfrak{S}}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\upsilon_{k,\mathfrak{S}}^{\ast}Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})}
be the exceptional divisor of
\upsilon_{k,\mathfrak{S}}
, where
Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})}
is the closed subscheme of
X_{k}
with the ideal sheaf
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})
.
Definitely,
Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})}
is the scheme of base points of
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})
.
Hence
E_{k,\mathfrak{S}}
is the scheme of base points of
\upsilon_{k,\mathfrak{S}}^{\ast}\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})
,
which implies that
\widehat{\mathfrak{W}}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=(\upsilon_{k,\mathfrak{S}}^{\ast}\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S}))_{\setminus E_{k,\mathfrak{S}}}
is base point free.

Seeing that
\upsilon_{k,\mathfrak{S}}
resolves
\varpi_{k,\mathfrak{S}}
, along with
\mu_{k,\mathfrak{S}}
, we obtain a commutative diagram as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{X}_{k,\mathfrak{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gr(k,\mathfrak{S}^{\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vee$}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{P}(\bigwedge^{k+1}\mathbb{S}^{\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vee$}}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\mu}_{k,\mathfrak{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\upsilon_{k,\mathfrak{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pluc}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{k,\mathfrak{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varpi_{k,\mathfrak{S}}}

Denote the lifting of
f_{[k]}
to
\widehat{X}_{k,\mathfrak{S}}
by
\hat{f}_{[k]}
.
Thus (
7.8
) yields

\tilde{f}_{k}=\hat{\mu}_{k,\mathfrak{S}}\circ\hat{f}_{[k]}.

Let
\mathcal{O}_{\Gr}(1)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\Pluc^{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N_{k}}}(1)
be the hyperplane line bundle on
\Gr(k,\mathfrak{S}^{\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vee$}})
.
It is evident that

(7.9)

\widehat{\mathfrak{W}}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})=\hat{\mu}_{k,\mathfrak{S}}^{\ast}|\mathcal{O}_{\Gr}(1)|,

and

(7.10)

\upsilon_{k,\mathfrak{S}}^{\ast}(\mathcal{O}_{X_{k}}(\bm{a}^{k})\otimes\pi_{0,k}^{\ast}L^{k+1})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{X}_{k,\mathfrak{S}}}(-E_{k,\mathfrak{S}})\cong\hat{\mu}_{k,\mathfrak{S}}^{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\Gr}(1).

7.4.
Generalization of the infinitesimal Plücker formula

For each
1\leqslant k\leqslant N
, set

(7.11)

L_{k}=\upsilon_{k,\mathfrak{S}}^{\ast}(\mathcal{O}_{X_{k}}(\bm{a}^{k})\otimes\pi_{0,k}^{\ast}L^{k+1})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{X}_{k,\mathfrak{S}}}(-E_{k,\mathfrak{S}})

and
L_{0}=L
.
It follows from (
7.9
) and (
7.10
) that
\widehat{\mathfrak{W}}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})=|L_{k}|
, and for any global sections
\sigma_{0},\dots,\sigma_{k}\in H^{0}(X,L)
, there exists a global section
\widehat{\omega}(\sigma_{0},\dots,\sigma_{k})\in H^{0}(\widehat{X}_{k},L_{k})
such that
\widehat{\sigma}_{k,\mathfrak{S}}\cdot\widehat{\omega}(\sigma_{0},\dots,\sigma_{k})=\upsilon_{k,\mathfrak{S}}^{\ast}\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\sigma_{0},\dots,\sigma_{k})
for a canonical section
\widehat{\sigma}_{k,\mathfrak{S}}
of
\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{X}_{k}}(E_{k,\mathfrak{S}})
.
Consider the Fubini-Study metric on
\mathbb{P}^{N_{k}}
, which can be regarded as a Hermitian metric
\tilde{h}_{FS}
on hyperplane line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N_{k}}}(1)
.
We naturally get a canonical Hermitian metric
\Pluc^{\ast}\tilde{h}_{FS}
on
\mathcal{O}_{\Gr}(1)
.
What’s more,
\hat{\mu}_{k,\mathfrak{S}}^{\ast}\Pluc^{\ast}\tilde{h}_{FS}
is a smooth Hermitian metric on
L_{k}
,
whose weight function
\varphi_{\widehat{\mathfrak{W}}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})}
is defined as (
6.4
).
More concretely,

\varphi_{\widehat{\mathfrak{W}}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})}=\log\left(\sum_{0\leqslant i_{0}<\dots<i_{k}\leqslant n}\hskip-10.00002pt|\widehat{\omega}(\varsigma_{i_{0}},\dots,\varsigma_{i_{k}})|^{2}\right).

This implies that

(\tilde{f}^{k})^{\ast}\widetilde{\omega}_{k}=\hat{f}_{[k]}^{\ast}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}\varphi_{\widehat{\mathfrak{W}}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})},

where
\widetilde{\omega}_{k}
is the Kähler form associated to the Fubini-Study metric on
\mathbb{P}^{N_{k}}
.
In addition,
\varpi_{k,\mathfrak{S}}^{\ast}\tilde{h}_{FS}
1
1

1

Here the “pull-back” by a meromorphic map is defined as (2.3) in
[
Shi76
]
.
gives a singular Hermitian metric on
\mathcal{O}_{X_{k}}(\bm{a}^{k})\otimes\pi_{0,k}^{\ast}L^{k+1}
with the weight function
\varphi_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})}
.

Regarding
\varphi_{\mathfrak{W}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})}
as a function on
\mathbb{W}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})^{\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vee$}}\hookrightarrow\bigwedge^{k+1}\mathbb{C}^{N+1}
, we see from (
3.1
) and (
7.6
) that

(7.12)

|\widetilde{F}^{k}|^{2}=\exp(\varphi_{\mathfrak{W}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})}\circ f_{[k]})|f_{[k-1]}^{\prime}|^{2k^{\prime}}

holds on the complex plane except stationary points, where
f_{[k-1]}^{\prime}
is defined by (
2.12
).
Take account of the formula

f_{[k-2]}^{\prime}=\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\pi_{k-1}(f_{[k-1]})\cdot f_{[k-1]}^{\prime}.

By the construction of the line bundle morphism (
2.7
) and the definition of
\varGamma_{k}
, the above formula yields

(7.13)

f_{[k-2]}^{\prime}=(\sigma_{\varGamma_{k}}\circ f_{[k]})\cdot f_{[k-1]}^{\prime},

where
\sigma_{\varGamma_{k}}
is a canonical section of
\mathcal{O}_{X_{k}}(\varGamma_{k})
.
Thanks to (
3.2
) and (
7.13
), (
7.12
) can be also formulated as

(7.14)

|\widetilde{F}^{k}|^{2}=\exp(\varphi_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})}\circ f_{[k]})|f^{\prime}|^{2k}|f_{[1]}^{\prime}|^{2(k-1)}\cdots|f_{[k-1]}^{\prime}|^{2}.

Indeed, one can check that (
7.14
) holds even at stationary points.
Therefore, the infinitesimal Plücker formula

(\tilde{f}^{k})^{\ast}\widetilde{\omega}_{k}=\frac{|\widetilde{F}^{k-1}|^{2}|\widetilde{F}^{k+1}|^{2}}{|\widetilde{F}^{k}|^{4}}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}|z|^{2}

on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N_{k}}}(1)
is reformulated as

\hat{f}_{[k]}^{\ast}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}\varphi_{\widehat{\mathfrak{W}}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})}=\exp(\varphi_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k-1},\mathfrak{S})}\circ f_{[k-1]}+\varphi_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k+1},\mathfrak{S})}\circ f_{[k+1]}-2\varphi_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})}\circ f_{[k]})|f_{[k]}^{\prime}|^{2}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}|z|^{2},

where we make the conventions that
\varphi_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{-1},\mathfrak{S})}=0
and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{0},\mathfrak{S})=\mathfrak{S}
.
For shortness, we write

(7.15)

\gamma_{k,\mathfrak{S}}=\exp(\varphi_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k-1},\mathfrak{S})}\circ f_{[k-1]}+\varphi_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k+1},\mathfrak{S})}\circ f_{[k+1]}-2\varphi_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})}\circ f_{[k]})|f_{[k]}^{\prime}|^{2}

Using the Poincaré-Lelong formula, (
7.14
) leads to

\displaystyle\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}[\log|\widetilde{F}^{k}|^{2}]

\displaystyle=f_{[k]}^{\ast}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}[\varphi_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})}]+\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}(k-\ell)[Z_{f^{\prime}_{[\ell]}}]

\displaystyle=f_{[k]}^{\ast}[\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}\varphi_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})}]+f_{[k]}^{\ast}[Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})}]+\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}(k-\ell)[Z_{f^{\prime}_{[\ell]}}]

\displaystyle=\hat{f}_{[k]}^{\ast}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}[\varphi_{\widehat{\mathfrak{W}}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})}]+f_{[k]}^{\ast}[Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})}]+\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}(k-\ell)[Z_{f^{\prime}_{[\ell]}}].

Accordingly,

\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}[\log\gamma_{k,\mathfrak{S}}]=\hat{f}_{[k-1]}^{\ast}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}[\varphi_{\widehat{\mathfrak{W}}(X_{k-1},\mathfrak{S})}]+\hat{f}_{[k+1]}^{\ast}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}[\varphi_{\widehat{\mathfrak{W}}(X_{k+1},\mathfrak{S})}]-2\hat{f}_{[k]}^{\ast}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}[\varphi_{\widehat{\mathfrak{W}}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})}]+\frac{1}{2}[Z_{\gamma_{k,\mathfrak{S}}}],

where

\frac{1}{2}[Z_{\gamma_{k,\mathfrak{S}}}]=f_{[k-1]}^{\ast}[Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k-1},\mathfrak{S})}]+f_{[k+1]}^{\ast}[Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k+1},\mathfrak{S})}]-f_{[k]}^{\ast}2[Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})}]+[Z_{f^{\prime}_{[k]}}].

Now, consider the case where
k=0
. We hereinafter prefer to write
\gamma_{\mathfrak{S}}
rather than
\gamma_{0,\mathfrak{S}}
.
Thanks to the assumption
\Bs(\mathfrak{S})=\varnothing
,
\mu_{\mathfrak{S}}^{\ast}\tilde{h}_{FS}
gives a smooth Hermitian metric on
L
with the weight function
\varphi_{\mathfrak{S}}
.
In this case, the above formula is simplified as

\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}[\log\gamma_{\mathfrak{S}}]=\hat{f}_{[1]}^{\ast}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}[\varphi_{\widehat{\mathfrak{W}}(X_{1},\mathfrak{S})}]-2f^{\ast}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}[\varphi_{\mathfrak{S}}]+\frac{1}{2}[Z_{\gamma_{\mathfrak{S}}}],

where
\dfrac{1}{2}[Z_{\gamma_{\mathfrak{S}}}]=f_{[1]}^{\ast}[Z_{\mathfrak{w}(X_{1},\mathfrak{S})}]+[Z_{f^{\prime}}]
.
Consequently, we obtain

\mathcal{N}(\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}[\log\gamma_{\mathfrak{S}}],r)=T_{\hat{f}_{[1]}}(r,\upsilon_{1,\mathfrak{S}}^{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(1)\otimes\mathcal{O}(-E_{1,\mathfrak{S}}))+N_{f_{[1]}}(r,Z_{\mathfrak{w}(X_{1},\mathfrak{S})})+\mathcal{N}([Z_{f^{\prime}}],r).

In particular, if
\mathfrak{S}
separates 1-jets (e.g.,
\mathfrak{S}=|L|
for some very ample line bundle
L
), then both
\varpi_{1,\mathfrak{S}}
and
\mu_{1,\mathfrak{S}}
are holomorphic.
Moreover,
Z_{\mathfrak{w}(X_{1},\mathfrak{S})}=\varnothing
,
E_{1,\mathfrak{S}}=\varnothing
and
\upsilon_{1,\mathfrak{S}}
is indeed the identity map.
In this case, we get

(7.16)

\mathcal{N}(\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}[\log\gamma_{\mathfrak{S}}],r)=T_{f_{[1]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(1))+\mathcal{N}([Z_{f^{\prime}}],r).

On the other hand, without loss of generality, suppose that
\gamma_{\mathfrak{S}}(0)\neq 0
, otherwise we can deal with it in the similar way to Remark
6.13
.
By the Green-Jensen formula, Jensen inequality and Lemma
6.29
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}(\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}[\log\gamma_{\mathfrak{S}}],r)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\int_{0}^{2\pi}\log\gamma_{\mathfrak{S}}(re^{i\theta})\frac{\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\theta}{2\pi}-\log\gamma_{\mathfrak{S}}(0)\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\log\int_{0}^{2\pi}\gamma_{\mathfrak{S}}(re^{i\theta})\frac{\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\theta}{2\pi}-\frac{1}{2}\log\gamma_{\mathfrak{S}}(0)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{(1+\delta)^{2}}{2}\log\mathcal{N}([\gamma_{\mathfrak{S}}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}|z|^{2}],r)-\left.\frac{1}{2}\log\gamma_{\mathfrak{S}}(0)\right\rVert_{E}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{(1+\delta)^{2}}{2}\log T_{f}(r,L)-\left.\frac{1}{2}\log\gamma_{\mathfrak{S}}(0)\right\rVert_{E}.

Especially, if
f
is an algebraic curve, we see that
\gamma_{\mathfrak{S}}(re^{i\theta})\leqslant O(1)
as
r\to\infty
.
Thus

\mathcal{N}(\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}[\log\gamma_{\mathfrak{S}}],r)=\frac{1}{2}\left(\int_{0}^{2\pi}\log\gamma_{\mathfrak{S}}(re^{i\theta})\frac{\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\theta}{2\pi}-\log\gamma_{\mathfrak{S}}(0)\right)\leqslant O(1)\quad(r\to\infty).

Combined with (
7.16
), these inequalities yield

(7.17)

T_{f_{[1]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(1))+\mathcal{N}([Z_{f^{\prime}}],r)\leqslant S_{f}(r).

Observe that
\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(1)\otimes\pi_{1}^{\ast}A
is ample for some sufficiently ample line bundle
A
on
X
.
According to (
7.17
) and Proposition
6.31
,
S_{f_{[1]}}(r)\leqslant S_{f}(r)
.
By induction, for each
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, hence we have

(7.18)

S_{f_{[k]}}(r)\leqslant S_{f}(r).

For each
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, think of
(X_{k},V_{k})
as the Demailly-Semple
1
-jet tower of
(X_{k-1},V_{k-1})
.
Applying (
7.17
) to
f_{[k-1]}
, we immediately get the following theorem.

Theorem 7.4
.

Let
(X,V)
be a directed projective manifold. Let
f\colon(\mathbb{C},T_{\mathbb{C}})\to(X,V)
be a non-constant holomorphic curve. For each
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, we have

T_{f_{[k]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{k}}(1))+\mathcal{N}([Z_{f_{[k-1]}^{\prime}}],r)\leqslant S_{f}(r).

7.5.
Ahlfors’ lemma on logarithmic derivative over directed manifolds

For any divisor
D\in\mathfrak{S}
, there exists a hyperplane
\widetilde{H}
of
\mathfrak{S}^{\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vee$}}\cong\mathbb{P}^{N}
such that
D=\mu_{\mathfrak{S}}^{\ast}\widetilde{H}
.
Let
\sigma
be the “unit” canonical section of
\mathcal{O}(D)
with respect to the basis
\{\varsigma_{i}\mid i=0,1,\dots,N\}
and let
\bm{\tilde{a}}\in(\mathbb{C}^{N+1})^{\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vee$}}
be a unit normal vector of
\widetilde{H}
.
By computation, we have

\widetilde{F}^{k}\,\llcorner\,\bm{\tilde{a}}=\sum_{0\leqslant i_{1}<\dots<i_{k}\leqslant n}\hskip-10.00002ptW(\sigma,\varsigma_{i_{1}},\dots,\varsigma_{i_{k}})(j_{k}f)\bm{e}^{i_{1}}\wedge\dots\wedge\bm{e}^{i_{k}}.

Analogous to (
7.14
), we have

(7.19)

|\widetilde{F}^{k}\,\llcorner\,\bm{\tilde{a}}|^{2}=\exp(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},D,\mathfrak{S})}\circ f_{[k]})|f^{\prime}|^{2k}|f_{[1]}^{\prime}|^{2(k-1)}\cdots|f_{[k-1]}^{\prime}|^{2},

where
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},D,\mathfrak{S})
is the linear system corresponding to
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},D,\mathfrak{S})
defined by (
5.2
).
By (
7.14
) and (
7.19
), the
k
-th contact function of
\tilde{f}
for
\widetilde{H}
is given by

(7.20)

\widetilde{\phi}_{k}(\widetilde{H})=\frac{|\widetilde{F}^{k}\,\llcorner\,\bm{\tilde{a}}|^{2}}{|\widetilde{F}^{k}|^{2}}=\exp\left((\varphi_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},D,\mathfrak{S})}-\varphi_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})})\circ f_{[k]}\right).

Notice that
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},D,\mathfrak{S})\subset\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})
, for any
D\in\mathfrak{S}
.
Therefore,

\upsilon_{k,\mathfrak{S}}^{-1}\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},D,\mathfrak{S})\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{X}_{k,\mathfrak{S}}}(E_{k,\mathfrak{S}})\subset\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{X}_{k,\mathfrak{S}}},

which is the ideal sheaf of a certain closed subscheme of
\widehat{X}_{k,\mathfrak{S}}
, denoted by
D_{\mathfrak{S}}^{\langle k\rangle}
.
Let
D_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]}
be the closed subscheme of
X_{k}
with the ideal sheaf
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},D,\mathfrak{S})
.
By definition,

(7.21)

D_{\mathfrak{S}}^{\langle k\rangle}+E_{k,\mathfrak{S}}=\upsilon_{k,\mathfrak{S}}^{\ast}D_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]}.

Take an arbitrary continuous Hermitian metric on
\mathcal{O}_{X_{k}}(\bm{a}^{k})\otimes\pi_{0,k}^{\ast}L^{k+1}
with the weight function
\varphi
.
It follows from (
6.5
) that
\dfrac{1}{2}(\varphi-\varphi_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})})
and
\dfrac{1}{2}(\varphi-\varphi_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},D,\mathfrak{S})})
are Weil functions for the closed subschemes
Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})}
and
D_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]}
of
X_{k}
, respectively.
Accordingly,
\dfrac{1}{2}(\varphi_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})}-\varphi_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},D,\mathfrak{S})})\circ\upsilon_{k,\mathfrak{S}}
is a Weil function for the closed subscheme
D_{\mathfrak{S}}^{\langle k\rangle}
of
\widehat{X}_{k,\mathfrak{S}}
.
Thanks to (
7.20
) , for any Weil function
\lambda_{D_{\mathfrak{S}}^{\langle k\rangle}}
for the closed subscheme
D_{\mathfrak{S}}^{\langle k\rangle}
, there exists some constant
a\geqslant 1
such that

\frac{1}{a}\widetilde{\phi}_{k}(\widetilde{H})\leqslant\!\exp\left(-2\lambda_{D_{\mathfrak{S}}^{\langle k\rangle}}\circ\hat{f}_{[k]}\right)\leqslant a\widetilde{\phi}_{k}(\widetilde{H}).

Consequently, applying Lemma
7.3
to holomorphic curves
\widetilde{f}=\mu_{\mathfrak{S}}\circ f
in
\mathfrak{S}^{\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vee$}}\cong\mathbb{P}^{N}
, we conclude Ahlfors’ lemma on logarithmic derivative over directed manifolds, which is formulated in the language of Weil functions for closed subschemes of Demailly-Semple jet towers.

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
(X,V)
be a directed projective manifold, and take an effective divisor
D
on
X
such that
|D|
is base point free.
Choose a linear system
\mathfrak{S}\subset|D|
satisfying that
D\in\mathfrak{S}
and
\mathfrak{S}
is also base point free.
For each
0\leqslant k\leqslant N-1
, let
f\colon(\mathbb{C},T_{\mathbb{C}})\to(X,V)
be a non-constant holomorphic curve such that
f_{[k]}(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Bs(\mathfrak{W}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S}))
.
Suppose that
f(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Supp D
.
Then there is a constant
c_{k}
such that for any
0<\varepsilon<1
, one has

\varepsilon\mathcal{N}\!\left(\exp\!\left(2(1-\varepsilon)\lambda_{D_{\mathfrak{S}}^{\langle k\rangle}}\!\circ\!\hat{f}_{[k]}-2\lambda_{D_{\mathfrak{S}}^{\langle k+1\rangle}}\!\circ\!\hat{f}_{[k+1]}\right)\hat{f}_{[k]}^{\ast}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}\varphi_{\widehat{\mathfrak{W}}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})},r\right)\leqslant c_{k}T_{\hat{f}_{[k]}}(r,L_{k})+O(1),

where
N=\dim\mathfrak{S}
, and
L_{k}
is defined by (
7.11
) with
L=\mathcal{O}(D)
.
Here
c_{k}
merely depends on the choice of Weil functions.

Remark 7.6
.

In particular, if
\mathfrak{S}
separates
1
-jets at every point of
X
, then both
\varpi_{1,\mathfrak{S}}
and
\mu_{1,\mathfrak{S}}
are holomorphic.
Thus
\upsilon_{1,\mathfrak{S}}
is indeed the identity map and
E_{1,\mathfrak{S}}=\varnothing
.
According to (
7.21
),
D_{\mathfrak{S}}^{\langle 1\rangle}=\upsilon_{1,\mathfrak{S}}^{\ast}D_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]}
.
Furthermore, the discussion in the next section will show that
D_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]}
is independent of the choice of
\mathfrak{S}
, provided that
\mathfrak{S}
separates
1
-jets at every point of
X
.

7.6.
Jets of closed subschemes over Demailly-Semple jet towers

Let
(X,V)
be a directed manifold.
For a closed subscheme
Z
of
X
, we define the closed subscheme
Z^{(1)}
of
X_{1}
as follows.
In the case when
X
is affine, choose some functions
\zeta_{1},\dots,\zeta_{s}
generating
\mathcal{I}_{Z}(X)\subset\mathcal{O}_{X}(X)
, where
\mathcal{I}_{Z}
is the ideal sheaf of
Z
of
X
.
Then we define the closed subscheme
Z^{(1)}
of
X_{1}=\textnormal{P}(V)
determined by the homogeneous ideal

I(Z^{(1)})=(\zeta_{1},\dots,\zeta_{s},\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\zeta_{1},\dots,\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\zeta_{s}),

where we abusively write
\zeta_{i}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}_{U,V}^{[0]}\zeta_{i}=p_{1,V}^{\ast}\zeta_{i}
and
\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\zeta_{i}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}_{U,V}^{[1]}\zeta_{i}
for each
1\leqslant i\leqslant s
.
We see from the Leibniz rule that
Z^{(1)}
is well-defined, regardless of the choice of the generators of
\mathcal{I}_{Z}(X)
.
More generally, even when
X
is not affine, we can take an affine open covering
\{U_{\epsilon}\}_{\epsilon\in\Upsilon}
of
X
and construct the closed subschemes
(Z_{\restriction U_{\epsilon}})^{(1)}
of
\pi_{1}^{-1}(U_{\epsilon})=\textnormal{P}(V_{\restriction U_{\epsilon}})
.
By definition, these closed subschemes can be glued together into the closed subscheme
Z^{(1)}
of
X_{1}=\textnormal{P}(V)
.

For each
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, we inductively define

Z^{(k)}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=(Z^{(k-1)})^{(1)}

as a closed subscheme of
X_{k}=\textnormal{P}(V_{k-1})
.

If
Z
is reduced and non-singular, we easily check that
Z^{(1)}
is identified with the Demailly-Semple
1
-jet tower of
(Z,T_{Z}\cap V)
.
In other words,

Z^{(1)}=\textnormal{P}(T_{Z}\cap V).

Remark 7.7
.

Let
Z=Z_{1}+Z_{2}
with
Z_{1}\cap Z_{2}
is not empty. One can see that
Z_{1}^{(1)}+Z_{2}^{(1)}\subsetneqq Z^{(1)}
by basic computations.
In particular, through straightforward calculations, one also has

(7.22)

(\ell Z)^{(1)}=(\ell-1)\pi_{1}^{\ast}Z+Z^{(1)},

for each
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
.

What’s more, one obtains the following proposition by definition.

Proposition 7.8
.

Let
(X,V)
be a directed manifold, and let
Z_{1},\dots,Z_{q}
be closed subschemes of
X
. For each
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, we have

Z^{(k)}=\bigcap_{i=1}^{q}Z_{i}^{(k)},

where
Z=\bigcap_{i=1}^{q}Z_{i}
.

Proof.

Take an affine open subset
U\subset X
.
Suppose that
\zeta_{i,1},\dots,\zeta_{i,s_{i}}
is a system of generators of
\mathcal{I}_{Z_{i}}(U)\subset\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
, for each
1\leqslant i\leqslant q
.
By Definition
6.4
,
i
),

\mathcal{I}_{Z}(U)=(\zeta_{1,1},\dots,\zeta_{1,s_{1}},\dots,\zeta_{q,1},\dots,\zeta_{q,s_{q}}),

and by the definition of
Z^{(1)}
,

I((Z_{\restriction U})^{(1)})=(\zeta_{1,1},\dots,\zeta_{q,s_{q}},\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\zeta_{1,1},\dots,\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\zeta_{q,s_{q}}).

On the other hand,

I((Z_{i\restriction U})^{(1)})=(\zeta_{i,1},\dots,\zeta_{i,s_{i}},\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\zeta_{i,1},\dots,\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\zeta_{i,s_{i}}),

for each
1\leqslant i\leqslant q
.
Again, by Definition
6.4
,
i
),

I(\bigcap_{i=1}^{q}(Z_{i\restriction U})^{(1)})=(\zeta_{1,1},\dots,\zeta_{q,s_{q}},\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\zeta_{1,1},\dots,\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\zeta_{q,s_{q}}).

Hence
(Z_{\restriction U})^{(1)}=\bigcap_{i=1}^{q}(Z_{i\restriction U})^{(1)}
.
Notice that all such open subsets
U
give an affine open covering of
X
. Thus

Z^{(1)}=\bigcap_{i=1}^{q}Z_{i}^{(1)}.

Then the proposition is verified by induction.
∎

Let
Z
be a closed subscheme of
X
.
Choose a linear system
\mathfrak{S}\subset|L|
on
X
satisfying that there exist some divisors
D_{1},\dots,D_{s}\in\mathfrak{S}
such that
Z=\bigcap_{i=1}^{s}D_{i}
.
It amounts to saying that
Z
is the closed subscheme of
X
with the ideal sheaf
\mathcal{I}_{Z}=\mathfrak{s}_{\supset Z}
, which is the base ideal of
\mathfrak{S}_{\supset Z}
.
Then we denote by
Z_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]}
the closed subscheme of
X_{k}
with the ideal sheaf
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},Z,\mathfrak{S})
.
Analogous to Proposition
7.8
, one also has

Proposition 7.9
.

Let
(X,V)
be a directed manifold, and let
Z_{1},\dots,Z_{q}
be closed subschemes of
X
.
Choose a linear system
\mathfrak{S}\subset|L|
on
X
such that
\mathcal{I}_{Z_{i}}=\mathfrak{s}_{\supset Z_{i}}
for each
1\leqslant i\leqslant q
.
For each
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, we have

Z_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]}=\bigcap_{i=1}^{q}(Z_{i})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]},

where
Z=\bigcap_{i=1}^{q}Z_{i}
.

In addition, the following proposition indicates a relation between
Z^{(1)}
and
Z_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]}
.

Proposition 7.10
.

Let
(X,V)
be a directed manifold, and let
Z
be a closed subscheme of
X
.
Choose a linear system
\mathfrak{S}\subset|L|
on
X
such that
\mathcal{I}_{Z}=\mathfrak{s}_{\supset Z}
. Then

\mathcal{I}_{Z^{(1)}}\cdot\mathfrak{w}(X_{1},\mathfrak{S})\subset\mathfrak{w}(X_{1},Z,\mathfrak{S})\subset\mathcal{I}_{Z^{(1)}}.

That is to say,

Z^{(1)}\subset Z_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]}\subset Z^{(1)}+Z_{\mathfrak{w}(X_{1},\mathfrak{S})}.

Proof.

Choose an affine open subset
U\subset X
, on which
L_{\restriction U}
can be trivialized.
It suffices to show that

(Z_{\restriction U})^{(1)}\subset(Z_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]})_{\restriction\pi_{1}^{\!-\!1\!}(U)}\subset(Z_{\restriction U})^{(1)}+(Z_{\mathfrak{w}(X_{1},\mathfrak{S})})_{\restriction\pi_{1}^{\!-\!1\!}(U)}.

Take a trivialization of
L_{\restriction U}
, which associates
\sigma_{U}\in\mathcal{O}(U)
to a global section
\sigma\in H^{0}(X,L)
.
Choose a convenient basis
\{\varsigma_{i}\mid i=0,1,\dots,N\}
of
\mathbb{S}
such that
\{\varsigma_{i}\mid i=0,1,\dots,N^{\prime}\}
is a basis of
\mathbb{S}_{\supset Z}
,
where
N=\dim\mathfrak{S}
and
N^{\prime}=\dim\mathfrak{S}_{\supset Z}
.
Therefore, the closed subscheme
(Z_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]})_{\restriction\pi_{1}^{\!-\!1\!}(U)}
of
\pi_{1}^{-1}(U)=\textnormal{P}(V_{\restriction U})
is determined by the homogeneous ideal

I((Z_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]})_{\restriction\pi_{1}^{\!-\!1\!}(U)})=(W_{U}(\varsigma_{i^{\prime}},\varsigma_{i}))_{0\leqslant i^{\prime}\leqslant N^{\prime},\,0\leqslant i^{\prime}<i\leqslant N}.

Analogously, the closed subscheme
(Z_{\mathfrak{w}(X_{1},\mathfrak{S})})_{\restriction\pi_{1}^{\!-\!1\!}(U)}
of
\pi_{1}^{-1}(U)=\textnormal{P}(V_{\restriction U})
is determined by the homogeneous ideal

I((Z_{\mathfrak{w}(X_{1},\mathfrak{S})})_{\restriction\pi_{1}^{\!-\!1\!}(U)})=(W_{U}(\varsigma_{i},\varsigma_{j}))_{0\leqslant i<j\leqslant N}.

Seeing that
\varsigma_{0,U},\dots,\varsigma_{N,U}
generates
\mathfrak{s}_{\supset Z}(U)\subset\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
,
the closed subscheme
(Z_{\restriction U})^{(1)}
of
\pi_{1}^{-1}(U)=\textnormal{P}(V_{\restriction U})
is determined by the homogeneous ideal

I((Z_{\restriction U})^{(1)})=(\varsigma_{0,U},\dots,\varsigma_{N^{\prime},U},\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\varsigma_{0,U},\dots,\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\varsigma_{N^{\prime},U}).

Apparently, for any
0\leqslant i^{\prime}\leqslant N^{\prime}
and
0\leqslant i^{\prime}<i\leqslant N
,

W_{U}(\varsigma_{i^{\prime}},\varsigma_{i})\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\varsigma_{i^{\prime},U}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\varsigma_{i,U}-\varsigma_{i,U}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\varsigma_{i^{\prime},U}\in I((Z_{\restriction U})^{(1)}),

which implies that

I((Z_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]})_{\restriction\pi_{1}^{\!-\!1\!}(U)})\subset I((Z_{\restriction U})^{(1)}).

In other words,

(Z_{\restriction U})^{(1)}\subset(Z_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]})_{\restriction\pi_{1}^{\!-\!1\!}(U)}.

On the other hand, we assert that

(Z_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]})_{\restriction\pi_{1}^{\!-\!1\!}(U)}\subset(Z_{\restriction U})^{(1)}+(Z_{\mathfrak{w}(X_{1},\mathfrak{S})})_{\restriction\pi_{1}^{\!-\!1\!}(U)},

which amounts to saying that

I((Z_{\restriction U})^{(1)})\cdot I((Z_{\mathfrak{w}(X_{1},\mathfrak{S})})_{\restriction\pi_{1}^{\!-\!1\!}(U)})\subset I((Z_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]})_{\restriction\pi_{1}^{\!-\!1\!}(U)}).

It suffices to verify that

\varsigma_{i^{\prime},U}W_{U}(\varsigma_{i},\varsigma_{j}),\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\varsigma_{i^{\prime},U}W_{U}(\varsigma_{i},\varsigma_{j})\in I((Z_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]})_{\restriction\pi_{1}^{\!-\!1\!}(U)}),

for any
0\leqslant i^{\prime}\leqslant N^{\prime}
and
0\leqslant i<j\leqslant N
.
It is obvious if
i\leqslant i^{\prime}
.
If
0\leqslant i^{\prime}<i<j\leqslant N
, then

\displaystyle\varsigma_{i^{\prime},U}W_{U}(\varsigma_{i},\varsigma_{j})

\displaystyle=\varsigma_{i,U}W_{U}(\varsigma_{i^{\prime}},\varsigma_{j})-\varsigma_{j,U}W_{U}(\varsigma_{i^{\prime}},\varsigma_{i})\in I((Z_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]})_{\restriction\pi_{1}^{\!-\!1\!}(U)}),

\displaystyle\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\varsigma_{i^{\prime},U}W_{U}(\varsigma_{i},\varsigma_{j})

\displaystyle=\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\varsigma_{i,U}W_{U}(\varsigma_{i^{\prime}},\varsigma_{j})-\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\varsigma_{j,U}W_{U}(\varsigma_{i^{\prime}},\varsigma_{i})\in I((Z_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]})_{\restriction\pi_{1}^{\!-\!1\!}(U)}).\qed

In the particular case where
\mathfrak{S}
separates
1
-jets at every point of
X
, it follows from Lemma
3.1
that
\mathfrak{w}(X_{1},\mathfrak{S})=\mathcal{O}_{X}
.
Accordingly, we immediately get the following corollary.

Corollary 7.11
.

Let
(X,V)
and
Z
be as above.
Choose a linear system
\mathfrak{S}
on
X
such that
\mathfrak{S}
separates
1
-jets at every point of
X
and
\mathcal{I}_{Z}=\mathfrak{s}_{\supset Z}
. Then

\mathfrak{w}(X_{1},Z,\mathfrak{S})=\mathcal{I}_{Z^{(1)}},

namely,

Z_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]}=Z^{(1)}.

Next, we are going to show that one can always find a linear system
\mathfrak{S}
satisfying the condition of Corollary
7.11
.

Lemma 7.12
.

[
Sil87
]

Let
Z
be a closed subscheme of
X
, then there exist effective Cartier divisors
D_{1},\dots,D_{s}
such that

Z=\bigcap_{i=1}^{s}D_{i}.

Lemma 7.13
.

Let
(X,V)
be a directed projective manifold. Given an arbitrary closed subscheme
Z
of
X
, we can find a linear system
\mathfrak{S}
on
X
such that
\mathfrak{S}
separates
1
-jets at every point of
X
and
\mathcal{I}_{Z}=\mathfrak{s}_{\supset Z}
.

Proof.

According to Lemma
7.12
, there exist effective divisors
D_{1},\dots,D_{s}
such that
Z=\bigcap_{i=1}^{s}D_{i}
.
Take a very ample line bundle
A
.
For each integer
1\leqslant i\leqslant s
, there is a positive integer
m_{i}
such that
A^{m}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(-D_{i})
is global generated, for every integer
m\geqslant m_{i}
.
Let
L=A^{m_{0}}
and
\mathfrak{S}=|L|
, where
\displaystyle m_{0}=\mathop{\max}_{1\leqslant i\leqslant s}m_{i}
.
Obviously,
\mathfrak{S}
separates
1
-jets at every point of
X
, and
\mathcal{I}_{D_{i}}=\mathfrak{s}_{\supset D_{i}}
for each
1\leqslant i\leqslant s
.
By Definition
6.4
,
i
), we see that

\mathcal{I}_{Z}=\mathfrak{s}_{\supset D_{1}}+\dots+\mathfrak{s}_{\supset D_{s}}\subset\mathfrak{s}_{\supset Z}.

On the other hand, it is evident that
\mathfrak{s}_{\supset Z}\subset\mathcal{I}_{Z}
.
Consequently,
\mathcal{I}_{Z}=\mathfrak{s}_{\supset Z}
.
∎

7.7.
General form of Ahlfors’ LLD over directed manifolds

Let us apply Lemma
7.5
with
k=0
.
Given an effective divisor
D
on
X
, observe that
\mathcal{I}_{D}=\mathfrak{s}_{\supset D}
, provided that
D\in\mathfrak{S}
.
Remark
7.6
and Corollary
7.11
lead to a corollary of Lemma
7.5
as follows.

Corollary 7.14
.

Let
(X,V)
be a directed projective manifold, and take a very ample divisor
D
on
X
.
Choose a linear system
\mathfrak{S}\subset|D|
satisfying that
D\in\mathfrak{S}
and
\mathfrak{S}
separates
1
-jets at every point of
X
.
Let
f\colon(\mathbb{C},T_{\mathbb{C}})\to(X,V)
be a non-constant holomorphic curve such that
f(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Supp D
.
Then there is a constant
c
such that the inequality

\varepsilon\mathcal{N}\left(\exp\left(2(1-\varepsilon)\lambda_{D}\circ f-2\lambda_{D^{(1)}}\circ f_{[1]}\right)f^{\ast}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}\varphi_{\mathfrak{S}},r\right)\leqslant cT_{f}(r,L)+O(1)

holds for any
0<\varepsilon<1
, where
L=\mathcal{O}(D)
.

Moreover, we can generalize Corollary
7.14
to closed subschemes of
X
.

Lemma 7.15
.

Let
(X,V)
be a directed projective manifold, and let
Z
be a closed subscheme of
X
.
Let
f\colon(\mathbb{C},T_{\mathbb{C}})\to(X,V)
be a non-constant holomorphic curve such that
f(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Supp Z
.
Choose a linear system
\mathfrak{S}\subset|L|
on
X
satisfying that
\mathfrak{S}
separates
1
-jets at every point of
X
and
\mathcal{I}_{Z}=\mathfrak{s}_{\supset Z}
.
Then there exists a constant
c>0
such that the inequality

(7.23)

\varepsilon\mathcal{N}\left(\exp\left(2(1-\varepsilon)\lambda_{Z}\circ f-2\lambda_{Z^{(1)}}\circ f_{[1]}\right)f^{\ast}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}\varphi_{\mathfrak{S}},r\right)\leqslant cT_{f}(r,L)+O(1)

holds for any
0<\varepsilon<1
.

Proof.

Since
f(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Supp Z
, we can find a basis
\{\varsigma_{i}\mid i=0,1,\dots,N^{\prime}\}
of
\mathbb{S}_{\supset Z}
such that
f(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Supp D_{i}
for each
0\leqslant i\leqslant N^{\prime}
, where
D_{i}=(\varsigma_{i})
.
According to Corollary
7.14
, for any
0<\varepsilon<1
, we have

\varepsilon\mathcal{N}\left(\exp\left(2(1-\varepsilon)\lambda_{D_{i}}\circ f-2\lambda_{D_{i}^{(1)}}\circ f_{[1]}\right)f^{\ast}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}\varphi_{\mathfrak{S}},r\right)\leqslant c^{\prime}T_{f}(r,L)+O(1).

We see from the assumption
\mathcal{I}_{Z}=\mathfrak{s}_{\supset Z}
that

Z=\bigcap_{0\leqslant i\leqslant N^{\prime}}D_{i}.

Furthermore, by Proposition
7.8
, we also obtain

Z^{(1)}=\bigcap_{0\leqslant i\leqslant N^{\prime}}D_{i}^{(1)}.

It follows from Proposition
6.5
,
i
) that

\displaystyle(1-\varepsilon)\lambda_{Z}\circ\pi_{1}-\lambda_{Z^{(1)}}

\displaystyle=_{\langle X_{1}\rangle}(1-\varepsilon)\mathop{\min}_{0\leqslant i\leqslant N^{\prime}}\lambda_{D_{i}}\circ\pi_{1}-\mathop{\min}_{0\leqslant i\leqslant N^{\prime}}\lambda_{D_{i}^{(1)}}

\displaystyle\leqslant_{\langle X_{1}\rangle}\mathop{\max}_{0\leqslant i\leqslant N^{\prime}}\left((1-\varepsilon)\lambda_{D_{i}}\circ\pi_{1}-\lambda_{D_{i}^{(1)}}\right),

which amounts to saying that

(1-\varepsilon)\lambda_{Z}\circ f-\lambda_{Z^{(1)}}\circ f_{[1]}\leqslant\mathop{\max}_{0\leqslant i\leqslant N^{\prime}}\left((1-\varepsilon)\lambda_{D_{i}}\circ f-\lambda_{D_{i}^{(1)}}\circ f_{[1]}\right)+a,

for some constant
a\geqslant 0
.

Hence we get

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mathcal{N}\left(\exp\left(2(1-\varepsilon)\lambda_{Z}\circ f-2\lambda_{Z^{(1)}}\circ f_{[1]}\right)f^{\ast}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}\varphi_{\mathfrak{S}},r\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\,

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mathcal{N}\left(\exp\left(\mathop{\max}_{0\leqslant i\leqslant N^{\prime}}\left(2(1-\varepsilon)\lambda_{D_{i}}\circ f-2\lambda_{D_{i}^{(1)}}\circ f_{[1]}\right)+2a\right)f^{\ast}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}\varphi_{\mathfrak{S}},r\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\,

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mathcal{N}\left(e^{2a}\mathop{\max}_{0\leqslant i\leqslant N^{\prime}}\exp\left(2(1-\varepsilon)\lambda_{D_{i}}\circ f-2\lambda_{D_{i}^{(1)}}\circ f_{[1]}\right)f^{\ast}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}\varphi_{\mathfrak{S}},r\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\,

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mathcal{N}\left(e^{2a}\sum_{i=0}^{N^{\prime}}\exp\left(2(1-\varepsilon)\lambda_{D_{i}}\circ f-2\lambda_{D_{i}^{(1)}}\circ f_{[1]}\right)f^{\ast}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}\varphi_{\mathfrak{S}},r\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\,

\displaystyle\varepsilon e^{2a}\sum_{i=0}^{N^{\prime}}\mathcal{N}\left(\exp\left(2(1-\varepsilon)\lambda_{D_{i}}\circ f-2\lambda_{D_{i}^{(1)}}\circ f_{[1]}\right)f^{\ast}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}\varphi_{\mathfrak{S}},r\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\,

\displaystyle c^{\prime}(N^{\prime}+1)e^{2a}T_{f}(r,L)+O(1).

The lemma follows by taking
c=c^{\prime}(N^{\prime}+1)e^{2a}
.
∎

What’s more, for arbitrary closed subschemes
Z_{1},\dots,Z_{q}
of
X
, we see that

(1-\varepsilon)\mathop{\max}_{1\leqslant i\leqslant q}\lambda_{Z_{i}}\circ\pi_{1}-\mathop{\max}_{1\leqslant i\leqslant q}\lambda_{Z_{i}^{(1)}}\leqslant\mathop{\max}_{1\leqslant i\leqslant q}\left((1-\varepsilon)\lambda_{Z_{i}}\circ\pi_{1}-\lambda_{Z_{i}^{(1)}}\right).

Following the same reasoning as in the proof of Lemma
7.15
, we conclude the following lemma from the above inequality.

Lemma 7.16
.

Let
(X,V)
be a directed projective manifold, and let
Z_{1},\dots,Z_{q}
be closed subschemes of
X
. Let
f\colon(\mathbb{C},T_{\mathbb{C}})\to(X,V)
be a non-constant holomorphic curve such that
f(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Supp(Z_{1}+\dots+Z_{q})
. For any linear system
\mathfrak{S}\subset|L|
on
X
satisfying that
\mathfrak{S}
separates
1
-jets at every point of
X
and
\mathcal{I}_{Z}=\mathfrak{s}_{\supset Z}
, there exists a constant
c>0
such that the inequality

\varepsilon\mathcal{N}\left(\exp\left(2(1-\varepsilon)\mathop{\max}_{1\leqslant i\leqslant q}\lambda_{Z_{i}}\circ f-2\mathop{\max}_{1\leqslant i\leqslant q}\lambda_{Z_{i}^{(1)}}\circ f_{[1]}\right)f^{\ast}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}\varphi_{\mathfrak{S}},r\right)\leqslant cT_{f}(r,L)+O(1)

holds for any
0<\varepsilon<1
.

We refer to this lemma as General form of Ahlfors’ Lemma on Logarithmic Derivative over Directed Manifolds (GALLDD for short).

8.
Algebro-geometric version of Ahlfors’ Lemma on Logarithmic Derivative

8.1.
Proof of AALD

First, we are going to prove AALD for a closed subscheme
Z
of
X
for
1
-jets.

Theorem 8.1
.

Let
(X,V)
be a directed projective manifold, and let
Z
be a closed subscheme of
X
.
Let
f\colon(\mathbb{C},T_{\mathbb{C}})\to(X,V)
be a non-constant holomorphic curve such that
f(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Supp Z
.
Then we have

m_{f}(r,Z)+T_{f_{[1]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(1))+\mathcal{N}([Z_{f^{\prime}}],r)\leqslant m_{f_{[1]}}(r,Z^{(1)})+S_{f}(r).

Proof.

Let
\gamma\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}|z|^{2}
denote the integrand of left hand side of (
7.23
), namely,

\gamma=\exp\left(2((1-\varepsilon)\lambda_{Z})\circ f-2\lambda_{Z^{(1)}}\circ f_{[1]}\right)\gamma_{\mathfrak{S}},

where
\mathfrak{S}\subset|L|
is a linear system on
X
such that
\mathfrak{S}
separates
1
-jets at every point of
X
, and
\mathcal{I}_{Z}=\mathfrak{s}_{\supset Z}
.

Without loss of generality, suppose that
\gamma_{\mathfrak{S}}(0)\neq 0
, otherwise we can deal with it in the similar way to Remark
6.13
.
Using the Green-Jensen formula and (
7.16
) , we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\log\gamma(re^{i\theta})\frac{\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\theta}{2\pi}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\!(1-\varepsilon)\lambda_{Z}\circ f(re^{i\theta})\frac{\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\theta}{2\pi}-\int_{0}^{2\pi}\!\lambda_{Z^{(1)}}\circ f_{[1]}(re^{i\theta})\frac{\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\theta}{2\pi}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\!\log\gamma_{\mathfrak{S}}(re^{i\theta})\frac{\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\theta}{2\pi}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\varepsilon)m_{f}(r,Z)-m_{f_{[1]}}(r,Z^{(1)})+\mathcal{N}(\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}[\log\gamma_{\mathfrak{S}}],r)+\frac{1}{2}\log\gamma_{\mathfrak{S}}(0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\varepsilon)m_{f}(r,Z)-m_{f_{[1]}}(r,Z^{(1)})+T_{f_{[1]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(1))+\mathcal{N}([Z_{f^{\prime}}],r)+\frac{1}{2}\log\gamma_{\mathfrak{S}}(0).

On the other hand, it follows from the Jensen inequality, Lemma
6.29
and Lemma
7.15
that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\log\gamma(re^{i\theta})\frac{\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\theta}{2\pi}

\displaystyle\leqslant\log\int_{0}^{2\pi}\gamma(re^{i\theta})\frac{\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\theta}{2\pi}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left.(1+\delta)^{2}\log\mathcal{N}([\gamma\mathop{}\!\textnormal{dd}^{c}|z|^{2}],r)\right\rVert_{E}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left.(1+\delta)^{2}\left(\log T_{f}(r,L)-\log\varepsilon+O(1)\right)\right\rVert_{E}.

Especially, if
f
is an algebraic curve, we claim that
\gamma(re^{i\theta})\leqslant O(1)
as
r\to\infty
.
Seeing that
\gamma_{\mathfrak{S}}(re^{i\theta})\leqslant O(1)\,(r\to\infty)
in the case of algebraic curves, it suffices to verify
\exp\left(2((1-\varepsilon)\lambda_{Z})\circ f(re^{i\theta})-2\lambda_{Z^{(1)}}\circ f_{[1]}(re^{i\theta})\right)\leqslant O(1)\,(r\to\infty)
.
Notice that the preimage of
Z
under an algebraic curve
f
is a finite set of points.
One easily find a constant
r_{0}>0
such that the poles of
\exp\left(\lambda_{Z}\circ f\right)
are all contained in the disk
\bigtriangleup(r_{0})
.
Our claim is evidently true, if
f
does not approach
Z
as
z\to\infty
.
By computation, one can check that
\exp\left(\lambda_{Z^{(1)}}\circ f\right)
also takes poles at
\infty
with at least the same order as
\exp\left(\lambda_{Z}\circ f\right)
, if
f
approaches
Z
as
z\to\infty
.
This implies that
\exp\left(2((1-\varepsilon)\lambda_{Z})\circ f(re^{i\theta})-2\lambda_{Z^{(1)}}\circ f_{[1]}(re^{i\theta})\right)\leqslant O(1)\,(r\to\infty)
.
Therefore, our claim holds, and we have

\int_{0}^{2\pi}\log\gamma(re^{i\theta})\frac{\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\theta}{2\pi}\leqslant O(1)\quad(r\to\infty).

Combining the above inequalities, we obtain

m_{f}(r,Z)+T_{f_{[1]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(1))+\mathcal{N}([Z_{f^{\prime}}],r)\leqslant\varepsilon m_{f}(r,Z)+m_{f_{[1]}}(r,Z^{(1)})+O\left(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)+S_{f}(r).

Since
\mathcal{I}_{Z}=\mathfrak{s}_{\supset Z}
and
f(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Supp Z
, there always exists a divisor
D\in\mathfrak{S}_{\supset Z}\subset|L|
such that
Z\subset D
and
f(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Supp D
.
Accordingly,

m_{f}(r,Z)\leqslant m_{f}(r,D)+O(1)\leqslant T_{f}(r,L)+O(1).

Take
\varepsilon(r)=\dfrac{1}{T_{f}(r,L)}
.
We thus see that

\varepsilon m_{f}(r,Z)+O\left(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)\leqslant S_{f}(r),

which verifies the theorem.
∎

What’s more, for each
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, think of
(X_{k},V_{k})
as the Demailly-Semple
1
-jet tower of
(X_{k-1},V_{k-1})
.
Applying Theorem
8.1
to
f_{[k-1]}
, we conclude Algebro-Geometric Version of Ahlfors’ Lemma on Logarithmic Derivative (AALD).

Theorem 8.2

(AALD)
.

Let
(X,V)
be a directed projective manifold, and let
Z
be a closed subscheme of
X
.
Let
f\colon(\mathbb{C},T_{\mathbb{C}})\to(X,V)
be a non-constant holomorphic curve such that
f(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Supp Z
.
For each
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, we have

m_{f_{[k-1]}}(r,Z^{(k-1)})+T_{f_{[k]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{k}}(1))+\mathcal{N}([Z_{f_{[k-1]}^{\prime}}],r)\leqslant m_{f_{[k]}}(r,Z^{(k)})+S_{f}(r).

Proof.

Observe that
f_{[k-1]}(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Supp\pi_{0,k-1}^{\ast}Z
.
Hence
f_{[k-1]}(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Supp Z^{(k-1)}
, since
\Supp Z^{(k-1)}\subset\Supp\pi_{0,k-1}^{\ast}Z
.
Applying Theorem
8.1
to
f_{[k-1]}
, we immediately get

m_{f_{[k-1]}}(r,Z^{(k-1)})+T_{f_{[k]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{k}}(1))+\mathcal{N}([Z_{f_{[k-1]}^{\prime}}],r)\leqslant m_{f_{[k]}}(r,Z^{(k)})+S_{f_{[k-1]}}(r).

Consequently, the theorem follows from (
7.18
).
∎

Remark 8.3
.

In the particular case when
Z=\varnothing
, Theorem
8.2
reduces to Theorem
7.4
.

8.2.
AALD with respect to a linear system

In this subsection, we will investigate what happens if the linear system might not separate the
1
-jets at every point of
X
.

Theorem 8.4
.

Let
(X,V)
be a directed projective manifold, and let
Z
be a closed subscheme of
X
.
Choose a linear system
\mathfrak{S}\subset|L|
on
X
satisfying that
\mathcal{I}_{Z}=\mathfrak{s}_{\supset Z}
.
Let
f\colon(\mathbb{C},T_{\mathbb{C}})\to(X,V)
be a non-constant holomorphic curve such that
f_{[1]}(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Bs(\mathfrak{W}(X_{1},\mathfrak{S}))
.
Suppose that
f(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Supp Z
.
Then,

m_{f}(r,Z)+T_{f_{[1]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(1))+\mathcal{N}([Z_{f^{\prime}}],r)\leqslant m_{f_{[1]}}(r,Z_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]})-m_{f}(r,Z_{\mathfrak{s}})+S_{f}(r),

where
Z_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]}
is the closed subscheme of
X_{k}
with the ideal sheaf
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},Z,\mathfrak{S})
.

Proof.

At first, if
\mathfrak{S}
is base point free, i.e.,
\Bs(\mathfrak{S})=\varnothing
, the theorem follows from Proposition
7.10
and Theorem
8.1
.

Next, consider the case when
\Bs(\mathfrak{S})\neq\varnothing
and
Z_{\mathfrak{s}}=D_{0}
for a certain effective Cartier divisor
D_{0}
.
Notice that
\mathfrak{s}_{\supset Z}\subset\mathfrak{s}
, namely,
D_{0}\subset Z
.
Hence there is a closed subscheme
Z_{1}
of
X
such that
Z=D_{0}+Z_{1}
.
Let
\sigma_{0}
be a canonical section of
\mathcal{O}_{X}(D_{0})
.
By computation, we obtain
W(\sigma_{0}\sigma_{1},\sigma_{0}\sigma_{2})=\sigma_{0}^{2}W(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
for any
\sigma_{1}\in(\mathbb{S}_{\setminus D_{0}})_{\supset Z_{1}}
and
\sigma_{2}\in\mathbb{S}_{\setminus D_{0}}
.
For any effective divisor
D
and any linear system
\mathfrak{S}
on
X
, let us use the notation
D\cdot\mathfrak{S}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\{D+D^{\prime}\mid D^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{S}\}
.
Thus

\mathfrak{W}(X_{1},Z,\mathfrak{S})=2\pi_{1}^{\ast}D_{0}\cdot\mathfrak{W}(X_{1},Z_{1},\mathfrak{S}_{\setminus D_{0}}).

It implies that

\hat{\pi}_{1}^{-1}\mathfrak{s}^{2}\cdot\mathfrak{w}(X_{1},Z_{1},\mathfrak{S}_{\setminus D_{0}})=\mathfrak{w}(X_{1},Z,\mathfrak{S}).

In other words,

(8.1)

Z_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]}=2\pi_{1}^{\ast}D_{0}+(Z_{1})_{\mathfrak{S}_{\setminus D_{0}}}^{[1]}.

Since
\mathfrak{S}_{\setminus D_{0}}
is base point free, we have

m_{f}(r,Z_{1})+T_{f_{[1]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(1))+\mathcal{N}([Z_{f^{\prime}}],r)\leqslant m_{f_{[1]}}(r,(Z_{1})_{\mathfrak{S}_{\setminus D_{0}}}^{[1]})+S_{f}(r).

Combined with (
8.1
), the above inequality yields

(8.2)

m_{f}(r,Z)+T_{f_{[1]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(1))+\mathcal{N}([Z_{f^{\prime}}],r)\leqslant m_{f_{[1]}}(r,Z_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]})-m_{f}(r,D_{0})+S_{f}(r).

Finally, let us investigate the case when
\Bs(\mathfrak{S})\neq\varnothing
and
Z_{\mathfrak{s}}
is not an effective Cartier divisor.
Take the blow-up of
(X,V)
with respect to
\mathfrak{s}

\upsilon\colon(\widehat{X},\widehat{V})\longrightarrow(X,V),

and denote by
E_{0}=\upsilon^{\ast}Z_{\mathfrak{s}}
the exceptional divisor of
\upsilon
.
Therefore, there is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\widehat{X}_{1},\widehat{V}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\widehat{X},\widehat{V})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{1},V_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X,V),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\pi}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\upsilon_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\upsilon}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}

where
\upsilon_{1}
is induced by the differential
\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\upsilon
restricted on
\widehat{V}
.
Obviously,
\upsilon_{1}
is holomorphic if and only if
\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\upsilon_{\restriction\widehat{V}}\colon\widehat{V}\to\upsilon^{\ast}V
is injective.
Accordingly,
\Ind(\upsilon_{1})=\textnormal{P}(T_{\widehat{X}/X}\cap\widehat{V})
.

Moreover, there is a resolution
\varrho\colon(\widetilde{X}_{1},\widetilde{V}_{1})\to(\widehat{X}_{1},\widehat{V}_{1})
of the rational map
\upsilon_{1}\colon\allowbreak(\widehat{X}_{1},\widehat{V}_{1})\dashrightarrow(X_{1},V_{1})
,
i.e.,
\varrho
is the composition of a finite number of blow-ups of directed manifolds with smooth centers such that the composition
\upsilon_{1}\circ\varrho
gives a holomorphic morphism
\widetilde{\upsilon}_{1}\colon(\widetilde{X}_{1},\widetilde{V}_{1})\to(X_{1},V_{1})
.
This amounts to the commutativity of the following diagram.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\widetilde{X}_{1},\widetilde{V}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\widehat{X}_{1},\widehat{V}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\widehat{X},\widehat{V})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{1},V_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X,V).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varrho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\upsilon}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\upsilon_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\pi}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\upsilon_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\upsilon}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}

Remark 8.5
.

Seeing that the composition of projective morphisms is also a projective morphism,

\varrho\colon\widetilde{X}_{1}\to\widehat{X}_{1}
is indeed a birational projective morphism of projective varieties.
According to Theorem 7.17 in Chapter II of
[
Har77
]
, there exists a coherent ideal sheaf
\mathcal{I}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{X}_{1}}
such that
\widetilde{X}_{1}
is isomorphic to the blow-up
\Bl_{\mathcal{I}}(\widehat{X}_{1})
of
\widehat{X}_{1}
.
Let
\widehat{U}_{1}\subset\widehat{X}_{1}
be the largest open subset such that
\varrho
restricted on
\varrho^{-1}(\widehat{U}_{1})
is an isomorphism.
Furthermore,
\mathcal{I}
can be chosen such that
\Supp(\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{X}_{1}}/\mathcal{I})=\widehat{X}_{1}\setminus\widehat{U}_{1}
, since
\widehat{X}_{1}
is non-singular
(cf. Exercises 7.11, (c) in Chapter II of
[
Har77
]
).

Analogous to the bundle morphism (
2.7
), there is a line bundle morphism over
\widetilde{X}_{1}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varrho^{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{X}_{1}}(-1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varrho^{\ast}\hat{\pi}_{1}^{\ast}\widehat{V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\upsilon}_{1}^{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(-1),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\upsilon}_{1}^{\ast}\upsilon_{\ast}}

where
\upsilon_{\ast}\colon\widehat{V}\to\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(-1)
.
And it admits
\widetilde{\varGamma}_{\upsilon}=\varrho^{\ast}\textnormal{P}(T_{\widehat{X}/X}\cap\widehat{V})\subset\widetilde{X}_{1}
as its zero divisor.
Consequently, we immediately get

(8.3)

\varrho^{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{X}_{1}}(1)=\widetilde{\upsilon}_{1}^{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(1)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}_{1}}(\widetilde{\varGamma}_{\upsilon}).

It is evident that
W(\sigma_{0}\circ\upsilon,\sigma_{1}\circ\upsilon)=W(\sigma_{0},\sigma_{1})\circ\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\upsilon_{\restriction\widehat{V}}
for any
\sigma_{0}\in\mathbb{S}_{\supset Z},\sigma_{1}\in\mathbb{S}
.
Hence
\omega(\sigma_{0}\circ\upsilon,\sigma_{1}\circ\upsilon)\circ\varrho=(\omega(\sigma_{0},\sigma_{1})\circ\widetilde{\upsilon}_{1})\widetilde{\gamma}
,
where
\widetilde{\gamma}
is a canonical section of
\mathcal{O}(\widetilde{\varGamma}_{\upsilon})
.
That is to say,

\varrho^{\ast}\mathfrak{W}(\widehat{X}_{1},\widehat{Z},\upsilon^{\ast}\mathfrak{S})=\widetilde{\varGamma}_{\upsilon}\cdot\widetilde{\upsilon}_{1}^{\ast}\mathfrak{W}(X_{1},Z,\mathfrak{S}),

where
\widehat{Z}=\upsilon^{\ast}Z
.
Thus

\varrho^{-1}\mathfrak{w}(\widehat{X}_{1},\widehat{Z},\upsilon^{\ast}\mathfrak{S})\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}_{1}}=\widetilde{\upsilon}_{1}^{-1}\mathfrak{w}(X_{1},Z,\mathfrak{S})\cdot\mathcal{I}_{\widetilde{\varGamma}_{\upsilon}},

which amounts to saying that

(8.4)

\varrho^{\ast}\widehat{Z}_{\upsilon^{\ast}\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]}=\widetilde{\upsilon}_{1}^{\ast}Z_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]}+\widetilde{\varGamma}_{\upsilon}.

Let
f\colon(\mathbb{C},T_{\mathbb{C}})\to(X,V)
be a holomorphic curve such that
f(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Supp Z
, and take the lifting
\hat{f}
of
f
to
(\widehat{X},\widehat{V})
.
Therefore,
\hat{f}\colon(\mathbb{C},T_{\mathbb{C}})\to(\widehat{X},\widehat{V})
is a holomorphic curve such that
f=\upsilon\circ\hat{f}
and
\hat{f}(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Supp\widehat{Z}
.
Observe that the scheme of base points of
\upsilon^{\ast}\mathfrak{S}
is the exceptional divisor
E_{0}
.
Applying (
8.2
) to
\hat{f}
, we obtain

(8.5)

m_{\hat{f}}(r,\widehat{Z})+T_{\hat{f}_{[1]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{X}_{1}}(1))+\mathcal{N}([\hat{f}^{\prime}],r)\leqslant m_{\hat{f}_{[1]}}(r,\widehat{Z}_{\upsilon^{\ast}\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]})-m_{\hat{f}}(r,E_{0})+S_{\hat{f}}(r),

where
\hat{f}_{[1]}
is the canonical lifting of
\hat{f}
to
(\widehat{X}_{1},\widehat{V}_{1})
.
Take the lifting
\tilde{f}_{[1]}
of
\hat{f}_{[1]}
to
(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{V})
.
Then
\hat{f}_{[1]}=\varrho\circ\tilde{f}_{[1]}
and
f_{[1]}=\widetilde{\upsilon}_{1}\circ\tilde{f}_{[1]}
.
Accordingly, (
8.3
) yields

(8.6)

T_{\hat{f}_{[1]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{X}_{1}}(1))=T_{f_{[1]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(1))+T_{\tilde{f}_{[1]}}(r,\widetilde{\varGamma}_{\upsilon}).

It follows from (
8.4
) that

(8.7)

m_{\hat{f}_{[1]}}(r,\widehat{Z}_{\upsilon^{\ast}\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]})=m_{f_{[1]}}(r,Z_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]})+m_{\tilde{f}_{[1]}}(r,\widetilde{\varGamma}_{\upsilon}).

Actually, by the definition of
\widetilde{\varGamma}_{\upsilon}
, we have
N_{\tilde{f}_{[1]}}(r,\widetilde{\varGamma}_{\upsilon})=\mathcal{N}([Z_{\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\upsilon(\hat{f})}],r)
.
Consequently,
f^{\prime}=\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\upsilon(\hat{f})\cdot\hat{f}^{\prime}
leads to

(8.8)

\mathcal{N}([Z_{f^{\prime}}],r)=N_{\tilde{f}_{[1]}}(r,\widetilde{\varGamma}_{\upsilon})+\mathcal{N}([Z_{\hat{f}^{\prime}}],r).

Seeing that
\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{X}}(-E_{0})
is relatively ample over
X
,
\upsilon^{\ast}A\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{X}}(-E_{0})
is ample for a sufficiently ample line bundle
A
on
X
.
We denote this ample line bundle on
\widehat{X}
by
\widehat{A}
.
Thanks to the fact that
\hat{f}(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Supp E_{0}
, we see that
T_{\hat{f}}(r,\widehat{A})\leqslant T_{f}(r,A)+O(1)
, which implies that

(8.9)

S_{\hat{f}}(r)\leqslant S_{f}(r).

Substituting (
8.6
)–(
8.9
) into (
8.5
) , we conclude that

m_{f}(r,Z)+T_{f_{[1]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(1))+\mathcal{N}([Z_{f^{\prime}}],r)\leqslant m_{f_{[1]}}(r,Z_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]})-m_{f}(r,Z_{\mathfrak{s}})+S_{f}(r).\qed

Remark 8.6
.

In the particular case when
\mathfrak{S}
separate the
1
-jets at every point of
X
, we immediately have
Z_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]}=Z^{(1)}
and
Z_{\mathfrak{s}}=\varnothing
.
It implies that Theorem
8.4
in this case coincides with Theorem
8.1
.

8.3.
General form of AALD

For arbitrary closed subschemes
Z_{1},\dots,Z_{q}
of
X
, following
[
RV20
]
, we will use the notation

\lambda_{\bigvee_{i=1}^{q}Z_{i}}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\mathop{\max}_{1\leqslant i\leqslant q}\lambda_{Z_{i}},

for the sake of brevity. Hence we obtain

(8.10)

m_{f}(r,\bigvee_{i=1}^{q}Z_{i})=\int_{0}^{2\pi}\!\mathop{\max}_{1\leqslant i\leqslant q}\lambda_{Z_{i}}\circ f(re^{i\theta})\frac{\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\theta}{2\pi}.

Here
\bigvee_{i=1}^{q}Z_{i}
is indeed a birational divisor, or b-divisor on
X
introduced by Shokurov
[
Sho96
]
.
For the precise definition and more details, see
[
Sho96
]
or Section 1.7 of
[
Cor07
]
.
We easily check that
\lambda_{\bigvee_{i=1}^{q}Z_{i}}\in\mathcal{C}(X)/=_{\langle X\rangle}
.
Actually, it is a generalized Weil function, or b-Weil function on
X
introduced by Vojta
[
Voj96
]
.

Following the same reasoning as in the proof of Theorem
8.1
, we conclude the following theorem from Lemma
7.16
.

Theorem 8.7
.

Let
(X,V)
be a directed projective manifold, and let
Z_{1},\dots,Z_{q}
be closed subschemes of
X
.
Let
f\colon(\mathbb{C},T_{\mathbb{C}})\to(X,V)
be a non-constant holomorphic curve such that
f(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Supp(Z_{1}+\dots+Z_{q})
. Then

m_{f}(r,\bigvee_{i=1}^{q}Z_{i})+T_{f_{[1]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(1))+\mathcal{N}([Z_{f^{\prime}}],r)\leqslant m_{f_{[1]}}(r,\bigvee_{i=1}^{q}Z_{i}^{(1)})+S_{f}(r).

Following the same reasoning as in the proof of Theorem
8.4
, Theorem
8.7
leads to the following theorem.

Theorem 8.8
.

Let
(X,V)
be a directed projective manifold, and let
Z_{1},\dots,Z_{q}
be closed subschemes of
X
.
Choose a linear system
\mathfrak{S}
on
X
satisfying that
\mathcal{I}_{Z_{i}}=\mathfrak{s}_{\supset Z_{i}}
for each
1\leqslant i\leqslant q
.
Let
f\colon(\mathbb{C},T_{\mathbb{C}})\to(X,V)
be a non-constant holomorphic curve such that
f_{[1]}(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Bs(\mathfrak{W}(X_{1},\mathfrak{S}))
.
Suppose that
f(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Supp(Z_{1}+\dots+Z_{q})
.
We thus have

m_{f}(r,\bigvee_{i=1}^{q}Z_{i})+T_{f_{[1]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(1))+\mathcal{N}([Z_{f^{\prime}}],r)\leqslant m_{f_{[1]}}(r,\bigvee_{i=1}^{q}(Z_{i})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]})-m_{f}(r,Z_{\mathfrak{s}})+S_{f}(r).

Lemma 8.9
.

Let
Z_{1},\dots,Z_{q}
be arbitrary closed subschemes of
X
. Then

\mathop{\max}_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell\end{subarray}}\sum_{i\in I}\lambda_{Z_{i}}=_{\langle X\rangle}\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\mathop{\max}_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=j\end{subarray}}\lambda_{Z_{I}}

holds for each
1\leqslant\ell\leqslant q
, where
|I|
denotes the cardinality of
I
, and we hereinafter write
J=\{1,2,\dots,q\}
, and
Z_{I}=\bigcap_{i\in I}Z_{i}
for
I\subset J
.

In addition, let
\ell_{0}
be the maximum of
|I|
for all the subsets
I\subset J
satisfying that
Z_{I}\neq\varnothing
. Then

\sum_{i=1}^{q}\lambda_{Z_{i}}=_{\langle X\rangle}\mathop{\max}_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell_{0}\end{subarray}}\sum_{i\in I}\lambda_{Z_{i}}.

Proof.

According to Proposition
6.5
,
i
), we get

\mathop{\max}_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell\end{subarray}}\sum_{i\in I}\lambda_{Z_{i}}=_{\langle X\rangle}\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\mathop{\max}_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=j\end{subarray}}\mathop{\min}_{i\in I}\lambda_{Z_{i}}=_{\langle X\rangle}\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\mathop{\max}_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=j\end{subarray}}\lambda_{Z_{I}}.

Notice that
Z_{I}=\varnothing
for all the subsets
I\subset J
with
|I|>\ell_{0}
.
It implies that
\mathop{\min}_{i\in I}\lambda_{Z_{i}}
is a continuous function on
X
, for all the subsets
I\subset J
with
|I|>\ell_{0}
.

\sum_{i=1}^{q}\lambda_{Z_{i}}=_{\langle X\rangle}\sum_{j=1}^{q}\mathop{\max}_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=j\end{subarray}}\mathop{\min}_{i\in I}\lambda_{Z_{i}}=_{\langle X\rangle}\sum_{j=1}^{\ell_{0}}\mathop{\max}_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=j\end{subarray}}\mathop{\min}_{i\in I}\lambda_{Z_{i}}=_{\langle X\rangle}\mathop{\max}_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell_{0}\end{subarray}}\sum_{i\in I}\lambda_{Z_{i}}.\qed

Thanks to Theorem
8.7
and Lemma
8.9
, we conclude General form of Algebro-geometric Version of Ahlfors’ Lemma on Logarithmic Derivative (GAALD for short) for
1
-jets.

Theorem 8.10

(GAALD for
1
-jets)
.

Let
(X,V)
be a directed projective manifold, and let
Z_{1},\dots,Z_{q}
be closed subschemes of
X
.
Take an integer
1\leqslant\ell\leqslant q
.
Let
f\colon(\mathbb{C},T_{\mathbb{C}})\to(X,V)
be a non-constant holomorphic curve such that
f(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Supp(Z_{1}+\dots+Z_{q})
. Then,

m_{f}(r,\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell\end{subarray}}\sum_{i\in I}Z_{i})+T_{f_{[1]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(\ell))+\ell\mathcal{N}([Z_{f^{\prime}}],r)\leqslant m_{f_{[1]}}(r,\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell\end{subarray}}\sum_{i\in I}Z_{i}^{(1)})+S_{f}(r).

Proof.

It follows from Lemma
8.9
and Proposition
7.8
that

(8.11)

\displaystyle m_{f}(r,\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell\end{subarray}}\sum_{i\in I}Z_{i})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}m_{f}(r,\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=j\end{subarray}}Z_{I})

(8.12)

\displaystyle m_{f}(r,\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell\end{subarray}}\sum_{i\in I}Z_{i}^{(1)})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}m_{f}(r,\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=j\end{subarray}}\bigcap_{i\in I}Z_{i}^{(1)})=\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}m_{f}(r,\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=j\end{subarray}}Z_{I}^{(1)}).

By Theorem
8.7
, for each
1\leqslant j\leqslant q
, we have

m_{f}(r,\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=j\end{subarray}}Z_{I})+T_{f_{[1]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(1))+\mathcal{N}([Z_{f^{\prime}}],r)\leqslant m_{f}(r,\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=j\end{subarray}}Z_{I}^{(1)})+S_{f}(r).

Combining all these inequalities for
1\leqslant j\leqslant\ell
, we get

\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}m_{f}(r,\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=j\end{subarray}}Z_{I})+T_{f_{[1]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(\ell))+\ell\mathcal{N}([Z_{f^{\prime}}],r)\leqslant\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}m_{f}(r,\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=j\end{subarray}}Z_{I}^{(1)})+S_{f}(r).

Substituting (
8.11
) and (
8.12
) into the above inequality, the theorem is verified.
∎

Likewise, we obtain the following theorem from Theorem
8.8
and Lemma
8.9
, and call it GAALD for
1
-jets with respect to linear system
\mathfrak{S}
.

Theorem 8.11
.

Let
(X,V)
be a directed projective manifold, and let
Z_{1},\dots,Z_{q}
be closed subschemes of
X
.
Choose a linear system
\mathfrak{S}
on
X
satisfying that
\mathcal{I}_{Z_{i}}=\mathfrak{s}_{\supset Z_{i}}
for each
1\leqslant i\leqslant q
.
Take an integer
1\leqslant\ell\leqslant q
.
Let
f\colon(\mathbb{C},T_{\mathbb{C}})\to(X,V)
be a non-constant holomorphic curve such that
f_{[1]}(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Bs(\mathfrak{W}(X_{1},\mathfrak{S}))
.
Suppose that
f(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Supp(Z_{1}+\dots+Z_{q})
.
Then we have

\displaystyle m_{f}(r,\!\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell\end{subarray}}\!\sum_{i\in I}Z_{i})+T_{f_{[1]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(\ell))+\ell\mathcal{N}([Z_{f^{\prime}}],r)

\displaystyle\leqslant\,

\displaystyle m_{f_{[1]}}\big(r,\!\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell\end{subarray}}\!\sum_{i\in I}(Z_{i})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]}\big)-\ell m_{f}(r,Z_{\mathfrak{s}})+S_{f}(r).

9.
Applications

In this section, we are going to show the applications of GAALD and its transform, and get the second main theorem type results for holomorphic curves.

9.1.
Applications of GAALD

For arbitrary closed subschemes
Z_{1},\dots,Z_{q}
of
X
, set

\ell_{k}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\max\{|I|\mid I\subset J,\,\bigcap_{i\in I}Z_{i}^{(k)}\neq\varnothing\}.

We easily see that
\ell_{k}
is a non-increasing sequence for
k
.
Therefore, we conclude the following theorem from GAALD.

Theorem 9.1
.

Let
(X,V)
be a directed projective manifold, and let
Z_{1},\dots,Z_{q}
be closed subschemes of
X
.
For each
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, let
f\colon(\mathbb{C},T_{\mathbb{C}})\to(X,V)
be a non-constant holomorphic curve such that
f(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Supp(Z_{1}+\dots+Z_{q})
.
Then,

\sum_{i=1}^{q}m_{f}(r,Z_{i})+T_{f_{[k]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{k}}(\bm{\ell}))+\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\ell_{j}\mathcal{N}([Z_{f_{[j]}^{\prime}}],r)\leqslant m_{f_{[k]}}(r,\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell_{k}\end{subarray}}\sum_{i\in I}Z_{i}^{(k)})+S_{f}(r),

where
\bm{\ell}=(\ell_{0},\dots,\ell_{k-1})\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{k}
and
\ell_{j}
is as above for each
0\leqslant j\leqslant k
.

Proof.

Observe that
f_{[j-1]}(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Supp\pi_{0,j-1}^{\ast}(Z_{1}+\dots+Z_{q})
, for every
1\leqslant j\leqslant k
.
Hence
f_{[j-1]}(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Supp(Z_{1}^{(j-1)}+\dots+Z_{q}^{(j-1)})
, since
\Supp(Z_{1}^{(j-1)}+\dots+Z_{q}^{(j-1)})\subset\Supp\pi_{0,j-1}^{\ast}(Z_{1}+\dots+Z_{q})
.
Think of
(X_{j},V_{j})
as the Demailly-Semple
1
-jet tower of
(X_{j-1},V_{j-1})
and apply Theorem
8.10
to
f_{[j-1]}
with
\ell=\ell_{j-1}
.
According to Lemma
8.9
and (
7.18
),

\displaystyle m_{f}(r,\hskip-5.0pt\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell_{j-1}\end{subarray}}\hskip-5.0pt\sum_{i\in I}Z_{i}^{(j-1)})+T_{f_{[j]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(\ell_{j-1}))+\ell_{j-1}\mathcal{N}([Z_{f_{[j-1]}^{\prime}}],r)

\displaystyle\leqslant\,

\displaystyle m_{f_{[j]}}(r,\hskip-5.0pt\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell_{j-1}\end{subarray}}\hskip-5.0pt\sum_{i\in I}Z_{i}^{(j)})+S_{f_{[j-1]}}(r)\leqslant m_{f_{[j]}}(r,\!\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell_{j}\end{subarray}}\!\sum_{i\in I}Z_{i}^{(j)})+S_{f}(r)

Combining all the inequalities for
1\leqslant j\leqslant k
, we thus have

m_{f}(r,\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell_{0}\end{subarray}}\sum_{i\in I}Z_{i})+T_{f_{[k]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{k}}(\bm{\ell}))+\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\ell_{j}\mathcal{N}([Z_{f_{[j]}^{\prime}}],r)\leqslant m_{f_{[k]}}(r,\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell_{k}\end{subarray}}\sum_{i\in I}Z_{i}^{(k)})+S_{f}(r).

Therefore, the theorem follows from
\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{q}m_{f}(r,Z_{i})=m_{f}(r,\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell_{0}\end{subarray}}\sum_{i\in I}Z_{i})
.
∎

Next, recall the simple normal crossings divisors on a complex manifold
X
.
One says a divisor
D=\sum_{i=1}^{q}D_{i}
is simple normal crossing, if
D
is normal crossing and each irreducible component
D_{i}
is non-singular.
In particular, the hyperplanes
H_{1},\dots,H_{q}
of
\mathbb{P}^{n}
are said to be in general position, if

\dim\bigcap_{i\in I}H_{i}\leqslant n-|I|,

for any subset
I\subset J
, where
\dim\varnothing=-\infty
by convention.
Obviously, the hyperplanes
H_{1},\dots,H_{q}
of
\mathbb{P}^{n}
are in general position, if and only if
D=\sum_{i=0}^{q}H_{i}
is a simple normal crossings divisor on
\mathbb{P}^{n}
.

For the simple normal crossings divisor
D
in general, we easily check that
\ell_{0}=n
, and
\ell_{j}=n-1
for each
j\geqslant 1
.
So is it, even for the hyperplanes of
\mathbb{P}^{n}
in general position.
On the other hand, the Wronskian in
\mathbb{P}^{n}
is indeed associated to the unique global section
\overline{\omega}\in H^{0}(X_{n},\mathcal{O}_{X_{n}}(\bm{a}^{n})\otimes\pi_{0,n}^{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{n}}(n+1))
.
In the next section, we will show that, compared with Theorem
9.1
, Theorem
8.11
is a more convenient tool to deal with SMT type questions.

9.2.
Applications of GAALD with respect to a linear system

Let
Z_{1}
and
Z_{2}
be two closed subschemes of
X
.
Take a linear system
\mathfrak{S}\subset|L|
on
X
such that both
\mathfrak{S}_{\supset Z_{1}}
and
\mathfrak{S}_{\supset Z_{2}}
are non-empty.
For any positive integer
k
, we introduce the notation
\mathfrak{W}(X_{k},Z_{1},Z_{2},\mathfrak{S})
and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},Z_{1},Z_{2},\mathfrak{S})
for the linear systems on
X_{k}
corresponding to the linear subspace

\mathbb{W}(X_{k},Z_{1},Z_{2},\mathfrak{S})\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\Span\{\omega(\sigma_{0},\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{k})\mid\sigma_{0}\in\mathbb{S}_{\supset Z_{1}},\,\sigma_{1}\in\mathbb{S}_{\supset Z_{2}},\,\sigma_{2},\dots,\sigma_{k}\in\mathbb{S}\}

of
H^{0}(X_{k},\mathcal{O}_{X_{k}}(k^{\prime})\otimes\pi_{0,k}^{\ast}L^{k+1})
, and the linear subspace

\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},Z_{1},Z_{2},\mathfrak{S})\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\Span\{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\sigma_{0},\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{k})\mid\sigma_{0}\in\mathbb{S}_{\supset Z_{1}},\,\sigma_{1}\in\mathbb{S}_{\supset Z_{2}},\,\sigma_{2},\dots,\sigma_{k}\in\mathbb{S}\}

of
H^{0}(X_{k},\mathcal{O}_{X_{k}}(\bm{a}^{k})\otimes\pi_{0,k}^{\ast}L^{k+1})
, respectively.
Moreover, we denote the base ideal sheaf of
\mathfrak{W}(X_{k},Z_{1},Z_{2},\mathfrak{S})
and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},Z_{1},Z_{2},\mathfrak{S})
by
\mathfrak{w}(X_{k},Z_{1},Z_{2},\mathfrak{S})
and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},Z_{1},Z_{2},\mathfrak{S})
, respectively.

Remark 9.2
.

We see that
\mathbb{W}(X_{k},D,D,\mathfrak{S})=0
and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},D,D,\mathfrak{S})=0
, for any divisor
D\in\mathfrak{S}
.

Now, we are going to investigate the closed subscheme of
X_{k}
with the ideal sheaf
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},Z_{1},Z_{2},\mathfrak{S})
, denoted by
(Z_{1},Z_{2})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]}
.
We get the following lemmas.

Lemma 9.3
.

Let
Z
be a closed subscheme of
X
.
Let
\mathfrak{S}\subset|L|
be linear systems on
X
satisfying that
\mathfrak{S}_{\supset Z}\neq\varnothing
.
For any
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
and for each
D\in\mathfrak{S}
except
D=Z
, we have

\displaystyle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}((X_{1})_{k},\mathfrak{W}(X_{1},D,\mathfrak{S}))=\pi_{0,k+1}^{-1}\mathcal{I}_{D}^{k}\cdot\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k+1},D,\mathfrak{S}),

\displaystyle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}((X_{1})_{k},(D,Z)_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]},\mathfrak{W}(X_{1},D,\mathfrak{S}))=\pi_{0,k+1}^{-1}\mathcal{I}_{D}^{k}\cdot\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k+1},D,Z,\mathfrak{S}).

In other words,

\displaystyle Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}((X_{1})_{k},\mathfrak{W}(X_{1},D,\mathfrak{S}))}

\displaystyle=D_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k+1]}+k\pi_{0,k+1}^{\ast}D,

\displaystyle((D,Z)_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]})_{\mathfrak{W}(X_{1},D,\mathfrak{S})}^{[k]}

\displaystyle=(D,Z)_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k+1]}+k\pi_{0,k+1}^{\ast}D.

Proof.

Take an open subset
U\subset X
on which
L
is trivialized.
Let
\sigma
be a canonical section of
\mathcal{O}(D)
.
Applying the generalized Leibniz rule, for each
0\leqslant j\leqslant k
and for any
\varsigma\in\mathbb{S}\subset H^{0}(X,L)
,

\displaystyle\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[\ell]}W_{U}(\sigma,\varsigma)

\displaystyle=\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[\ell]}\left(\sigma_{U}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[1]}\varsigma-\varsigma_{U}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[1]}\sigma\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}\binom{\ell}{j}\left(\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[\ell-j]}\sigma\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[j+1]}\varsigma-\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[\ell-j]}\varsigma\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[j+1]}\sigma\right)

\displaystyle=\sigma_{U}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[\ell+1]}\varsigma-\varsigma_{U}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[\ell+1]}\sigma+\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\left(\binom{\ell}{j-1}-\binom{\ell}{j}\right)\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[\ell-j+1]}\sigma\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[j]}\varsigma,

where we abusively write
\varsigma_{U}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}_{U,V}^{[0]}\varsigma_{U}=p_{k,V}^{\ast}\varsigma_{U}
and
\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[j]}\varsigma\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}_{U,V}^{[j]}\varsigma_{U}
for each
0\leqslant j\leqslant\ell
.
Furthermore, take an affine open subset
U_{1}\subset\pi_{1}^{-1}(U)\subset X_{1}
