, and choose a local holomorphic section
\tau
of
\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(1)(\pi_{1}^{-1}(U))
such that
\tau
is nowhere zero on
U_{1}
, namely,
\tau_{\restriction U_{1}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\vee}\in\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(1)(U_{1})
.
We then obtain a local trivialization of
\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(1)(U_{1})
given by

\displaystyle\tau_{\restriction U_{1}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\vee}\otimes\colon\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(1)(U_{1})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}(U_{1})

\displaystyle\omega

\displaystyle\longmapsto\tau_{\restriction U_{1}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\vee}\otimes\omega.

Let
t
be a meromorphic function on the total space of
V_{\restriction U}
satisfying that
\dfrac{1}{t}=(\pi_{1})_{\ast}\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle\vee}
.
Apparently,
t
is holomorphic and nowhere zero on
\widetilde{U}_{1}
, where
\widetilde{U}_{1}
is an open subset of the total space of
V
such that
\textnormal{P}(\widetilde{U}_{1})=U_{1}
.
Thus the local trivializations of
L
and
\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(1)
induce a trivialization of
\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(1)\otimes\pi_{1}^{\ast}L^{2}
on
U_{1}
,
which associates
\omega_{U_{1}}(\sigma_{0},\sigma_{1})
to
\omega(\sigma_{0},\sigma_{1})\in H^{0}(X_{1},\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(1)\otimes\pi_{1}^{\ast}L^{2})
.
Consider the natural map

\displaystyle\pr_{k,\ell}\colon J_{k+\ell}V\setminus(X\times\{0\})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow J_{k}V_{\ell}

\displaystyle j_{k+\ell}f

\displaystyle\longmapsto j_{k}f_{[\ell]}.

We see that
\omega_{U_{1}}(\sigma_{0},\sigma_{1})\circ\pr_{0,1}=tW_{U}(\sigma_{0},\sigma_{1})
on
\widetilde{U}_{1}\setminus(U\times\{0\})
.
Therefore, for any
\varsigma_{0},\dots,\varsigma_{k}\in\mathbb{S}
, we get

\displaystyle W_{U_{1}}(\omega(\sigma,\varsigma_{0}),\dots,\omega(\sigma,\varsigma_{k}))\circ\pr_{k,1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t^{k+1}W_{\widetilde{U}_{1}\setminus(U\times\{0\})}(W_{U}(\sigma,\varsigma_{0}),\dots,W_{U}(\sigma,\varsigma_{k}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t^{k+1}\begin{vmatrix}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[0]}W_{U}(\sigma,\varsigma_{0})&\cdots&\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[0]}W_{U}(\sigma,\varsigma_{k})\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[k]}W_{U}(\sigma,\varsigma_{0})&\cdots&\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[k]}W_{U}(\sigma,\varsigma_{k})\end{vmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t^{k+1}\sigma_{U}^{-1}\begin{vmatrix}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[0]}\sigma&\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[0]}\varsigma_{0}&\cdots&\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[0]}\varsigma_{k}\\
0&\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[0]}W_{U}(\sigma,\varsigma_{0})&\cdots&\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[0]}W_{U}(\sigma,\varsigma_{k})\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
0&\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[k]}W_{U}(\sigma,\varsigma_{0})&\cdots&\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[k]}W_{U}(\sigma,\varsigma_{k})\end{vmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t^{k+1}\sigma_{U}^{-1}\begin{vmatrix}\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[0]}\sigma&\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[0]}\varsigma_{0}&\cdots&\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[0]}\varsigma_{k}\\
\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[0]}\sigma\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[1]}\sigma&\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[0]}\sigma\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[1]}\varsigma_{0}&\cdots&\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[0]}\sigma\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[1]}\varsigma_{k}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[0]}\sigma\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[k+1]}\sigma&\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[0]}\sigma\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[k+1]}\varsigma_{0}&\cdots&\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[0]}\sigma\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}^{[k+1]}\varsigma_{k}\end{vmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t^{k+1}\sigma_{U}^{k}W_{U}(\sigma,\varsigma_{0},\dots,\varsigma_{k}).

Notice that all such open subsets
U
give an open covering of
X
, and all such open subsets
U_{1}
give an affine open covering of
X_{1}
.
Accordingly,

\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\omega(\sigma,\varsigma_{0}),\dots,\omega(\sigma,\varsigma_{k}))=\pi_{0,k+1}^{\ast}\sigma^{k}\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\sigma,\varsigma_{0},\dots,\varsigma_{k}),

which amounts to saying that

\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}((X_{1})_{k},\mathfrak{W}(X_{1},D,\mathfrak{S}))=k\pi_{0,k+1}^{\ast}D\cdot\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k+1},D,\mathfrak{S}).

Consequently,

\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}((X_{1})_{k},\mathfrak{W}(X_{1},D,\mathfrak{S}))=\pi_{0,k+1}^{-1}\mathcal{I}_{D}^{k}\cdot\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k+1},D,\mathfrak{S}).

Observe that
\omega(\sigma,\varsigma_{0})\in\mathbb{W}(X_{1},D,Z,\mathfrak{S})
if and only if
\varsigma_{0}\in\mathbb{S}_{\supset Z}
.
Hence

\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}((X_{1})_{k},(D,Z)_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]},\mathfrak{W}(X_{1},D,\mathfrak{S}))=\pi_{0,k+1}^{-1}\mathcal{I}_{D}^{k}\cdot\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k+1},D,Z,\mathfrak{S}).\qed

We make a convention that
(D,Z)_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]}=X_{k}
if
D=Z
.
Using Crofton’s formula, we calculate the average of the proximity functions as follows.

Lemma 9.4
.

Let
(X,V)
be a directed projective manifold.
For each
0\leqslant k\leqslant N-1
, let
f\colon(\mathbb{C},T_{\mathbb{C}})\to(X,V)
be a non-constant holomorphic curve such that
f_{[k]}(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Bs(\mathfrak{W}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S}))
.
For any linear system
\mathfrak{S}\subset|L|
on
X
, we have

\int_{D\in\mathfrak{S}}\!m_{f}(r,D_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]})\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\rho(D)=m_{f}(r,Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})})+O(1),

where
N=\dim\mathfrak{S}
and
\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\rho(D)
is the unique measure on
\mathfrak{S}\cong(\mathbb{P}^{n})^{\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vee$}}
invariant under the unitary group and satisfying
\rho(\mathfrak{S})=1
.

Take a closed subscheme
Z
of
X
.
Choose a linear system
\mathfrak{S}\subset|L|
on
X
such that
\mathcal{I}_{Z}=\mathfrak{s}_{\supset Z}
.
Then

\int_{D\in\mathfrak{S}}\!m_{f}(r,(D,Z)_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]})\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\rho(D)=m_{f}(r,Z_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]})+O(1)

holds for any non-constant holomorphic curve satisfying that
f(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Supp Z
besides.
What’s more, let
Z_{1},\dots,Z_{q}
be closed subschemes of
X
.
Choose a linear system
\mathfrak{S}\subset|L|
on
X
such that
\mathcal{I}_{Z_{i}}=\mathfrak{s}_{\supset Z_{i}}
for each
1\leqslant i\leqslant q
.

\int_{D\in\mathfrak{S}}\!m_{f}(r,\!\bigvee_{i=1}^{q}(D,Z_{i})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]})\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\rho(D)=m_{f}(r,\!\bigvee_{i=1}^{q}(Z_{i})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]})+O(1)

holds for any non-constant holomorphic curve satisfying that
f(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Supp(Z_{1}+\dots+Z_{q})
besides.

Proof.

Let
\mathbb{S}\subset H^{0}(X,L)
be the linear subspace corresponding to
\mathfrak{S}
.
Take a basis
\{\varsigma_{i}\mid i=0,1,\dots,N\}
of
\mathbb{S}
.
For each array of indices
0\leqslant i_{1}<\dots<i_{k}\leqslant N
, set

\mathbb{S}_{i_{1},\dots,i_{k}}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\sigma,\varsigma_{i_{1}},\dots,\varsigma_{i_{k}})\mid\sigma\in\mathbb{S}\}.

Naturally, there is a surjective homomorphism

\displaystyle\beta_{i_{1},\dots,i_{k}}\colon\mathbb{S}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbb{S}_{i_{1},\dots,i_{k}}

\displaystyle\sigma

\displaystyle\longmapsto\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\sigma,\varsigma_{i_{1}},\dots,\varsigma_{i_{k}}),

for each array of indices
0\leqslant i_{1}<\dots<i_{k}\leqslant N
.
It is evident that

\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})=\hskip-10.00002pt\sum_{0\leqslant i_{1}<\dots<i_{k}\leqslant N}\hskip-10.00002pt\mathbb{S}_{i_{1},\dots,i_{k}},

and

Z_{\beta}(D)=\hskip-10.00002pt\bigcap_{0\leqslant i_{1}<\dots<i_{k}\leqslant N}\hskip-10.00002pt\beta_{i}(D)=D_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]},

for any
D\in\mathfrak{S}
, where
Z_{\beta}
is defined by (
6.11
).
Applying Theorem
6.26
and Remark
6.27
with
\mathfrak{T}=\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})
, and
\mathfrak{S}_{i_{1},\dots,i_{k}}
,
\mathfrak{S}
as above, we have

\int_{D\in\mathfrak{S}}\!m_{f}(r,D_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]})\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\rho(D)=m_{f}(r,Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})})+O(1).

In addition, choose a convenient basis
\{\varsigma_{i}\mid i=0,1,\dots,N\}
of
\mathbb{S}
such that
\{\varsigma_{i}\mid i=0,1,\dots,N^{\prime}\}
is a basis of
\mathbb{S}_{\supset Z}
,
where
N^{\prime}=\dim\mathfrak{S}_{\supset Z}
.
We easily check that

\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},Z,\mathfrak{S})=\hskip-10.00002pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leqslant i_{1}\leqslant N^{\prime}\\
0\leqslant i_{1}<\dots<i_{k}\leqslant N\end{subarray}}\hskip-10.00002pt\mathbb{S}_{i_{1},\dots,i_{k}},

and

Z_{\beta}(D)=\hskip-10.00002pt\bigcap_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leqslant i_{1}\leqslant N^{\prime}\\
0\leqslant i_{1}<\dots<i_{k}\leqslant N\end{subarray}}\hskip-10.00002pt\beta_{i}(D)=(D,Z)_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]},

for any
D\in\mathfrak{S}
.
It follows from Theorem
6.26
and Remark
6.27
that

\int_{D\in\mathfrak{S}}\!m_{f}(r,(D,Z)_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]})\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\rho(D)=m_{f}(r,Z_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]})+O(1).

Moreover, observe that

\lambda_{\bigvee_{i=1}^{q}Z_{i}}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\mathop{\max}_{1\leqslant i\leqslant q}\lambda_{Z_{i}}=\sum_{j=1}^{q}(-1)^{j-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=j\end{subarray}}\lambda_{Z_{I}}.

By the definition of proximity functions and Proposition
7.9
, these formulas above lead to

\displaystyle\int_{D\in\mathfrak{S}}\!m_{f}(r,\!\bigvee_{i=1}^{q}(D,Z_{i})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]})\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\rho(D)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{q}(-1)^{j-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=j\end{subarray}}\int_{D\in\mathfrak{S}}\!m_{f}(r,(D,Z_{I})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]})\mathop{}\!\textnormal{d}\rho(D)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{q}(-1)^{j-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=j\end{subarray}}m_{f}(r,(Z_{I})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]})+O(1)

\displaystyle=m_{f}(r,\!\bigvee_{i=1}^{q}(Z_{i})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]})+O(1).\qed

Remark 9.5
.

If
f\colon(\mathbb{C},T_{\mathbb{C}})\to(X,V)
is non-degenerated relative to
\mathfrak{S}
, then
f
satisfies all the non-degenerated condition in Lemma
9.4
.

By induction, we conclude the following theorem from Theorem
8.11
.

Theorem 9.6
.

Let
(X,V)
be a directed projective manifold, and let
Z_{1},\dots,Z_{q}
be closed subschemes of
X
.
Choose a linear system
\mathfrak{S}
on
X
satisfying that
\mathcal{I}_{Z_{i}}=\mathfrak{s}_{\supset Z_{i}}
for each
1\leqslant i\leqslant q
.
Take an integer
1\leqslant\ell\leqslant q
.
For each
1\leqslant k\leqslant N
, let
f\colon(\mathbb{C},T_{\mathbb{C}})\to(X,V)
be a non-constant holomorphic curve such that
f_{[k]}(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Bs(\mathfrak{W}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S}))
.
Suppose that
f(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Supp(Z_{1}+\dots+Z_{q})
. We have

\displaystyle m_{f_{[k-1]}}(r,\!\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell\end{subarray}}\!\sum_{i\in I}(Z_{i})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k-1]})+T_{f_{[k]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{k}}(\ell))+\ell\mathcal{N}([Z_{f_{[k-1]}^{\prime}}],r)

\displaystyle\leqslant\,

\displaystyle m_{f_{[k]}}(r,\!\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell\end{subarray}}\!\sum_{i\in I}(Z_{i})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]})-\ell m_{f_{[k-1]}}(r,Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k-1},\mathfrak{S})})+\ell m_{f_{[k-2]}}(r,Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k-2},\mathfrak{S})})+S_{f}(r),

where
N=\dim\mathfrak{S}
and we make the conventions that
(Z_{i})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[0]}=Z_{\mathfrak{s}_{\supset Z_{i}}}
,
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{0},\mathfrak{S})=\mathfrak{s}
, and
m_{f_{[-1]}}(r,Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{-1},\mathfrak{S})})=0
.

Proof.

If
k=1
, the theorem definitely follows from Theorem
8.11
.
In the case when
k=2
, think of
(X_{2},V_{2})
as the Demailly-Semple
1
-jet tower of
(X_{1},V_{1})
.
Take an arbitrary effective divisor
D\in\mathfrak{S}
.
For brevity, we write
(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{V})=(X_{1},V_{1})
,
\widetilde{\mathfrak{S}}=\mathfrak{w}(X_{1},D,\mathfrak{S})
, and
\widetilde{Z}_{i}=(D,Z_{i})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]}
for each
1\leqslant i\leqslant q
.
Notice that
\mathcal{I}_{\widetilde{Z}_{i}}=\widetilde{\mathfrak{S}}_{\supset\widetilde{Z}_{i}}
.
Applying Theorem
8.11
to
\tilde{f}=f_{[1]}
with respect to
\widetilde{\mathfrak{S}}
, we get

(9.1)

\displaystyle m_{\tilde{f}}(r,\!\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell\end{subarray}}\!\sum_{i\in I}\widetilde{Z}_{i})+T_{\tilde{f}_{[1]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}_{1}}(\ell))+\ell\mathcal{N}([Z_{\tilde{f}^{\prime}}],r)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle m_{\tilde{f}_{[1]}}\big(r,\!\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell\end{subarray}}\!\sum_{i\in I}(\widetilde{Z}_{i})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{S}}}^{[1]}\big)-\ell m_{\tilde{f}}(r,Z_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}})+S_{\tilde{f}}(r).

It follows from Lemma
9.3
and (
7.18
) that (
9.1
) is reformulated as

\displaystyle m_{f_{[1]}}\big(r,\!\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell\end{subarray}}\!\sum_{i\in I}(D,Z_{i})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]}\big)+T_{f_{[2]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{2}}(\ell))+\ell\mathcal{N}([Z_{f_{[1]}^{\prime}}],r)

\displaystyle\leqslant\,

\displaystyle\ell m_{f}(r,D)+m_{f_{[2]}}\big(r,\!\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell\end{subarray}}\!\sum_{i\in I}(D,Z_{i})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[2]}\big)-\ell m_{f_{[1]}}(r,Z_{\mathfrak{w}(X_{1},D,\mathfrak{S})})+S_{f}(r).

Taking the average of two sides for
D\in\mathfrak{S}
, by Lemma
9.4
, we conclude that

\displaystyle m_{f_{[1]}}(r,\!\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell\end{subarray}}\!\sum_{i\in I}(Z_{i})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[1]})+T_{f_{[2]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{2}}(\ell))+\ell\mathcal{N}([Z_{f_{[1]}^{\prime}}],r)

\displaystyle\leqslant\,

\displaystyle m_{f_{[2]}}(r,\!\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell\end{subarray}}\!\sum_{i\in I}(Z_{i})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[2]})-\ell m_{f_{[1]}}(r,Z_{\mathfrak{w}(X_{1},\mathfrak{S})})+\ell m_{f}(r,Z_{\mathfrak{s}})+S_{f}(r).

Assume the theorem establishes for
k=j
.
Analogous to the reasoning in the case when
k=2
, we are going to verify the theorem for
k=j+1
.
Applying the theorem with
k=j
to
\tilde{f}
with respect to
\widetilde{\mathfrak{S}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle m_{\tilde{f}_{[j-1]}}(r,\!\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell\end{subarray}}\!\sum_{i\in I}(\widetilde{Z}_{i})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{S}}}^{[j-1]})+T_{\tilde{f}_{[j]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}}(\ell))+\ell\mathcal{N}([Z_{\tilde{f}_{[j-1]}^{\prime}}],r)

\displaystyle\leqslant\,

\displaystyle m_{\tilde{f}_{[j]}}(r,\!\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell\end{subarray}}\!\sum_{i\in I}(\widetilde{Z}_{i})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{S}}}^{[j]})-\ell m_{\tilde{f}_{[j-1]}}(r,Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\widetilde{X}_{j-1},\widetilde{\mathfrak{S}})})+\ell m_{\tilde{f}_{[j-2]}}(r,Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\widetilde{X}_{j-2},\widetilde{\mathfrak{S}})})+S_{\tilde{f}}(r),

which amounts to saying that

\displaystyle m_{f_{[j]}}(r,\!\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell\end{subarray}}\!\sum_{i\in I}(D,Z_{i})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[j]})+T_{f_{[j+1]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{j+1}}(\ell))+\ell\mathcal{N}([Z_{f_{[j]}^{\prime}}],r)

\displaystyle\leqslant\,

\displaystyle m_{f_{[j+1]}}(r,\!\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell\end{subarray}}\!\sum_{i\in I}(D,Z_{i})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[j+1]})-\ell m_{f_{[j]}}(r,D_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[j]})+\ell m_{f_{[j-1]}}(r,D_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[j-1]})+S_{f}(r).

Taking the average of two sides for
D\in\mathfrak{S}
, by Lemma
9.4
, we have

\displaystyle m_{f_{[j]}}(r,\!\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell\end{subarray}}\!\sum_{i\in I}(Z_{i})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[j]})+T_{f_{[j+1]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{j+1}}(\ell))+\ell\mathcal{N}([Z_{f_{[j]}^{\prime}}],r)

\displaystyle\leqslant\,

\displaystyle m_{f_{[j+1]}}(r,\!\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell\end{subarray}}\!\sum_{i\in I}(Z_{i})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[j+1]})-\ell m_{f_{[j]}}(r,Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{j},\mathfrak{S})})+\ell m_{f_{[j-1]}}(r,Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{j-1},\mathfrak{S})})+S_{f}(r).\qed

For arbitrary closed subschemes
Z_{1},\dots,Z_{q}
of
X
, set

\ell_{k,\mathfrak{S}}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\max\{|I|\mid I\subset J,\,\bigcap_{i\in I}(Z_{i})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]}\supsetneqq Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})}\}.

We easily check that
\ell_{k,\mathfrak{S}}
is a non-increasing sequence for
k
.
Let
k_{\mathfrak{S}}
be the minimum of
k
such that
\ell_{k,\mathfrak{S}}=0
.
Since
\ell_{N,\mathfrak{S}}=0
, then
k_{\mathfrak{S}}
always exists and
k_{\mathfrak{S}}\leqslant N
.

Using the notation above, we get the following theorem.

Theorem 9.7
.

Let
(X,V)
be a directed projective manifold, and let
Z_{1},\dots,Z_{q}
be closed subschemes of
X
.
Choose a linear system
\mathfrak{S}
on
X
satisfying that
\mathcal{I}_{Z_{i}}=\mathfrak{s}_{\supset Z_{i}}
for each
1\leqslant i\leqslant q
.
Let
\ell_{k,\mathfrak{S}}
and
k_{\mathfrak{S}}
be as above.
Let
f\colon(\mathbb{C},T_{\mathbb{C}})\to(X,V)
be a non-constant holomorphic curve such that
f_{[k_{\mathfrak{S}}]}(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Bs(\mathfrak{W}(X_{k_{\mathfrak{S}}},\mathfrak{S}))
.
Suppose that
f(\mathbb{C})\not\subset\Supp(Z_{1}+\dots+Z_{q})
.
Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{q}m_{f}(r,Z_{i})+T_{f_{[k_{\mathfrak{S}}]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{k_{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\bm{\ell}_{\mathfrak{S}}))+\sum_{j=0}^{k_{\mathfrak{S}}-1}\ell_{j,\mathfrak{S}}\mathcal{N}([Z_{f_{[j]}^{\prime}}],r)

\displaystyle\leqslant\,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{k_{\mathfrak{S}}}(\ell_{j-1,\mathfrak{S}}-2\ell_{j,\mathfrak{S}}+\ell_{j+1,\mathfrak{S}})m_{f_{[j]}}(r,Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{j},\mathfrak{S})})+S_{f}(r),

where
\bm{\ell}_{\mathfrak{S}}=(\ell_{0,\mathfrak{S}},\dots,\ell_{k_{\mathfrak{S}}-1,\mathfrak{S}})\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{k_{\mathfrak{S}}}
and we make the conventions that
\ell_{-1,\mathfrak{S}}=q
, and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{0},\mathfrak{S})=\mathfrak{s}
.

Proof.

By definition, for each
\ell_{k,\mathfrak{S}}<\ell\leqslant q
, we have

\mathop{\max}_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\bigcap_{i\in I}(Z_{i})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]}}=_{\langle X\rangle}\lambda_{Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})}}.

According to Lemma
8.9
,

\sum_{i=1}^{q}m_{f}(r,Z_{i})=m_{f}(r,\hskip-10.00002pt\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell_{-1,\mathfrak{S}}\end{subarray}}\hskip-10.00002pt\sum_{i\in I}Z_{i})=m_{f}(r,\hskip-5.0pt\!\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell_{k,\mathfrak{S}}\end{subarray}}\hskip-5.0pt\!\sum_{i\in I}Z_{i})+(\ell_{-1,\mathfrak{S}}-\ell_{0,\mathfrak{S}})m_{f_{[k]}}(r,Z_{\mathfrak{s}})+O(1),

m_{f_{[k]}}(r,\hskip-10.00002pt\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell_{k-1,\mathfrak{S}}\end{subarray}}\hskip-10.00002pt\sum_{i\in I}(Z_{i})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]})=m_{f_{[k]}}(r,\hskip-5.0pt\!\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell_{k,\mathfrak{S}}\end{subarray}}\hskip-5.0pt\!\sum_{i\in I}(Z_{i})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]})+(\ell_{k-1,\mathfrak{S}}-\ell_{k,\mathfrak{S}})m_{f_{[k]}}(r,Z_{\mathfrak{w}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})})+O(1).

Applying Theorem
9.6
with
\ell=\ell_{k-1,\mathfrak{S}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle m_{f_{[k-1]}}(r,\hskip-10.00002pt\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell_{k-1,\mathfrak{S}}\end{subarray}}\hskip-10.00002pt\sum_{i\in I}(Z_{i})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k-1]})+T_{f_{[k]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{k}}(\ell_{k-1,\mathfrak{S}}))+\ell_{k-1,\mathfrak{S}}\mathcal{N}([Z_{f_{[k-1]}^{\prime}}],r)

\displaystyle\leqslant\,

\displaystyle m_{f_{[k]}}(r,\hskip-10.00002pt\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell_{k-1,\mathfrak{S}}\end{subarray}}\hskip-10.00002pt\sum_{i\in I}(Z_{i})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]})-\ell_{k-1,\mathfrak{S}}m_{f_{[k-1]}}(r,Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k-1},\mathfrak{S})})+\ell_{k-1,\mathfrak{S}}m_{f_{[k-2]}}(r,Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k-2},\mathfrak{S})})+S_{f}(r)

\displaystyle\leqslant\,

\displaystyle m_{f_{[k]}}(r,\hskip-5.0pt\!\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset J\\
|I|=\ell_{k,\mathfrak{S}}\end{subarray}}\hskip-5.0pt\!\sum_{i\in I}(Z_{i})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]})+(\ell_{k-1,\mathfrak{S}}-\ell_{k,\mathfrak{S}})m_{f_{[k-1]}}(r,Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})})

\displaystyle-\ell_{k-1,\mathfrak{S}}m_{f_{[k-1]}}(r,Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k-1},\mathfrak{S})})+\ell_{k-1,\mathfrak{S}}m_{f_{[k-2]}}(r,Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k-2},\mathfrak{S})})+S_{f}(r).

Combining all the inequalities for
1\leqslant k\leqslant k_{\mathfrak{S}}
, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{q}m_{f}(r,Z_{i})+T_{f_{[k_{\mathfrak{S}}]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{k_{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\bm{\ell}_{\mathfrak{S}}))+\sum_{j=0}^{k_{\mathfrak{S}}-1}\ell_{j,\mathfrak{S}}\mathcal{N}([Z_{f_{[j]}^{\prime}}],r)

\displaystyle\leqslant\,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{k_{\mathfrak{S}}}(\ell_{j-1,\mathfrak{S}}-2\ell_{j,\mathfrak{S}}+\ell_{j+1,\mathfrak{S}})m_{f_{[j]}}(r,Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{j},\mathfrak{S})})+S_{f}(r).\qed

Remark 9.8
.

It is evident that Theorem
9.7
still establishes for arbitrary
\ell_{0,\mathfrak{S}}^{\prime},\dots,\allowbreak\ell_{N,\mathfrak{S}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\ell_{k,\mathfrak{S}}\leqslant\ell_{k,\mathfrak{S}}^{\prime}
for each
0\leqslant k\leqslant N
.
In practice, we usually consider closed subschemes
Z_{1},\dots,Z_{q}
of
X
satisfying a certain condition.
Then we technically take
\ell_{k,\mathfrak{S}}
to be the maximum of
\ell_{k,\mathfrak{S}}
for all the finite sets of closed subschemes of
X
satisfying such condition.

9.3.
Second Main Theorem for some particular cases

Now, let us investigate the particular case where
(X,V)=(\mathbb{P}^{n},T_{\mathbb{P}^{n}})
and
Z_{1},\dots,Z_{q}
are the hyperplanes
H_{1},\dots,H_{q}
of
\mathbb{P}^{n}
in general position.
Then choose the complete linear system
\mathfrak{S}=|H_{1}|
.
Obviously,
\dim\mathbb{S}=n+1
and
\dim\mathbb{S}_{\supset\bigcap_{i\in I}H_{i}}>n-k+1
for all the subsets
I\subset J
with
|I|>n-k
.
We see from the pigeonhole principle that
\bigcap_{i\in I}(H_{i})_{\mathfrak{S}}^{[k]}=Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{k},\mathfrak{S})}
for all the subsets
I\subset J
with
|I|>n-k
, namely,
\ell_{k,\mathfrak{S}}\leqslant n-k
.
Hence we conclude Cartan’s second main theorem for holomorphic curves.

Theorem 9.9
.

[Cartan’s SMT]
Let
H_{1},\dots,H_{q}
be the hyperplanes of
\mathbb{P}^{n}
in general position.
For
f\colon\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{P}^{n}
be a linear non-degenerated holomorphic curve, we have

\sum_{i=1}^{q}m_{f}(r,H_{i})+\mathcal{N}([Z_{W(f)}],r)\leqslant T_{f}(r,\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{n}}(n+1))+S_{f}(r),

where the Wronskian
W(f)=F^{n}
defined by (
7.1
).

Proof.

Choose the linear system
\mathfrak{S}=|H_{1}|
as above.
Thus we can take
k_{\mathfrak{S}}=n
, and
\ell_{k,\mathfrak{S}}=n-k
.
Applying Theorem
9.7
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{q}m_{f}(r,H_{i})+T_{f_{[n]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{n}}(\bm{a}^{n}))+\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}(n-j)\mathcal{N}([Z_{f_{[j]}^{\prime}}],r)

\displaystyle\leqslant\,

\displaystyle(q-n-1)m_{f}(r,Z_{\mathfrak{s}})+m_{f_{[n]}}(r,Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{n},\mathfrak{S})})+S_{f}(r).

Since
\mathfrak{S}
is base point free,
Z_{\mathfrak{s}}=\varnothing
.
And
Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{n},\mathfrak{S})}
is indeed an effective divisor associated to the only
n
-th Wronskian global section
\overline{\omega}(\varsigma_{0},\dots,\varsigma_{n})\in H^{0}(X_{n},\mathcal{O}_{X_{n}}(\bm{a}^{n})\otimes\pi_{0,n}^{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{n}}(n+1))
.
That is to say,

(9.2)

T_{f_{[n]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{n}}(\bm{a}^{n}))+T_{f}(r,\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{n}}(n+1))=T_{f_{[n]}}(r,Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{n},\mathfrak{S})})+O(1).

Therefore,

\sum_{i=1}^{q}m_{f}(r,H_{i})+N_{f_{[n]}}(r,Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{n},\mathfrak{S})})+\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}(n-j)\mathcal{N}([Z_{f_{[j]}^{\prime}}],r)\leqslant T_{f}(r,\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{n}}(n+1))+S_{f}(r).

Observe that

\mathcal{N}([Z_{W(f)}],r)=N_{f_{[n]}}(r,Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{n},\mathfrak{S})})+\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}(n-j)\mathcal{N}([Z_{f_{[j]}^{\prime}}],r).

The theorem is verified.
∎

On the other hand, we easily see that
P^{(1)}=\varnothing
, if
P=\{p\}
is the set of a point in
X
.
According to Theorem
8.7
, we immediately obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 9.10
.

Let
(X,V)
be a directed projective manifold, and let
P_{1},\dots,P_{q}
be the distinct points in
X
.
Let
f\colon(\mathbb{C},T_{\mathbb{C}})\to(X,V)
be a non-constant holomorphic curve.
Then we have

\sum_{i=1}^{q}m_{f}(r,P_{i})+T_{f_{[1]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(1))+\mathcal{N}([Z_{f^{\prime}}],r)\leqslant S_{f}(r).

More specifically, if
X=\mathbb{P}^{n}
and
V=T_{\mathbb{P}^{n}}
, we get the second main theorem for holomorphic curves intersecting points in projective spaces.

Theorem 9.11
.

Let
P_{1},\dots,P_{q}
be the distinct points in
\mathbb{P}^{n}
.
For
f\colon\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{P}^{n}
be a linear non-degenerated holomorphic curve, we have

n\sum_{i=1}^{q}m_{f}(r,P_{i})+\frac{2}{n+1}\mathcal{N}([Z_{W(f)}],r)\leqslant T_{f}(r,\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{n}}(2))+S_{f}(r).

Proof.

Applying Corollary
9.10
to
f
, we obtain

(9.3)

n^{\prime}\sum_{i=1}^{q}m_{f}(r,P_{i})+T_{f_{[1]}}(r,\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}(n^{\prime}))+n^{\prime}\mathcal{N}([Z_{f^{\prime}}],r)\leqslant S_{f}(r),

where
n^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=1+\dots+n=\dfrac{n(n+1)}{2}
.

At the same time, it follows from (
7.13
) that

\mathcal{N}([Z_{f_{[j-1]}^{\prime}}],r)=N_{f_{[j+1]}}(r,\varGamma_{j+1})+\mathcal{N}([Z_{f_{[j]}^{\prime}}],r)

for each
1\leqslant j\leqslant n-1
.
Substituting all these equalities for
j=1,\dots,n-1
into (
9.3
), by (
9.2
), we get

n^{\prime}\sum_{i=1}^{q}m_{f}(r,P_{i})+N_{f_{[n]}}(r,Z_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(X_{n},\mathfrak{S})})+\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}(n-j)\mathcal{N}([Z_{f_{[j]}^{\prime}}],r)\leqslant T_{f}(r,\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{n}}(n+1))+S_{f}(r).

Therefore,

n^{\prime}\sum_{i=1}^{q}m_{f}(r,P_{i})+\mathcal{N}([Z_{W(f)}],r)\leqslant T_{f}(r,\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{n}}(n+1))+S_{f}(r).\qed
